- `OPENAI_MAX_RETRIES` / `OPENAI_FALLBACK_MAX_RETRIES` (env knobs, not flags; defaults 2 / inherit = byte-identical unset, `#117`) — explicit retry ceilings on all five `AsyncOpenAI` constructions so a 429 storm cannot multiply requests (`url_extraction_service.get_client` joined in session 71 by #285; issue #265 closed). `model_config.verdict_chain_max_attempts()` makes the worst case derivable (6 shipped default, 3 at the runbook launch values). Sizing arithmetic: `docs/runbooks/2026-09-02-openai-tpm-launch-sizing.md` — every derived figure labelled **MODELLED**, and the ~6.6-10 compares/min ceiling is CONDITIONAL on an UNVERIFIED Tier-1 assumption (verifying the real tier is a hard launch gate).
- `ENABLE_SPEC_FIELD_NORM` (default OFF, `#100`) — normalizes spec fields PER FIELD before aggregating instead of summing raw cross-unit magnitudes (a 5000 mAh battery, a 6.1 in display and 128 GB storage were added as bare numbers, so the biggest-magnitude field decided the dimension and a product could win on unit choice). A `>=10x` same-field gap ties at the midpoint as a unit/notation artifact. **Known residual:** that tie also erases a GENUINE same-unit 10x gap (16 GB vs 512 GB) — canary-watch.
- `ENABLE_MISSING_DIM_RENORM` (default OFF, `#101`) — renormalizes over dimensions actually present instead of injecting `MISSING_SCORE=50`, which let a product with NO data outscore one with genuinely poor measured data. **Two P1 inversions were found in review and fixed before merge** (the flag reintroduced the bug it targets): (a) renorm excludes exactly the dims the measured product wins, leaving parity, and the price-authority delta then penalised ONLY the product with data because an all-missing product was EXEMPT — price authority is now SYMMETRIC under this flag; (b) the effective set could reduce to nothing but both-sided-missing sentinels, so ZERO measured data decided the winner — that now falls back to the legacy sum. Parity ties prefer the product with MORE measured dims instead of argmax-on-index. **Known residual (canary-watch):** a mixed-provenance pair (measured product on an ESTIMATED price vs a rating-void on a TRUSTED price) still inverts — that is the authority feature doing its designed job, and closing it needs a redesign that abandons exclude-and-renormalize.
- **`CATEGORY_MIN_COVERAGE` is NOT inverted** — the M18 report claimed it was; verified against the code, `penalty_factor = 0.5 + coverage_ratio` applies only below `min_coverage` and INCREASES with coverage, so lower coverage takes the LARGER discount. The real "low coverage wins" effect is magnitude concentration, which is what `#100` fixes. No change made.

**Wave M13-W1 flags (front-door hardening — auth / rate-limit / paid-work gating, branch `feature/m14-wave1-closeout` from `674034e`, 2026-08-31).** M13 review Wave 1 landed findings `M13-01, M13-02, M13-03, M13-21, M13-25, M13-26, M13-28, M13-29`. Six are unflagged pure corrections (M13-21 openapi_url 404 in prod; M13-25 POST+GET body/query max_length; M13-26 image error-envelope; M13-28 referral limiter + Path/Query patterns, rides existing `ENABLE_REFERRAL_SYSTEM`; M13-29 UUID-validate `comparison_id` on BOTH /feedback and /events; M13-03 admin-dep on GET /text/price-kpi). Three NEW flags, all default OFF, all read PER CALL via `os.getenv` so flag-OFF is byte-identical to `674034e`:
- `ENABLE_DEFAULT_RATE_LIMITS` (default OFF) — gates the registration of `SlowAPIMiddleware` and thus the blanket `default_limits` (ANON_LIMIT=10/min) on the 21 previously-undecorated routes. **Effect ON:** every undecorated route gets a 10/min default. **Why dark:** under the shipped proxy-IP key the default is keyed on the shared Railway edge-proxy IP = ONE deployment-wide bucket per URL path, which 429s the hot app-open reads (`/app/version`, `/usage/status`, `/auth/me`, `/auth/verify`) and infra (`/health`, `/`, `/favicon.ico`) for every user. **PRECONDITION: flip ONLY together with a verified `ENABLE_PROXY_AWARE_RATELIMIT`** so the key is per-client, never the shared proxy IP. The credential-route brute-force protection (explicit `@limiter.limit("5/min")` + account-lockout on PUT /auth/{email,password}) is decorator-driven and is LIVE regardless of this flag.
- `ENABLE_PROXY_AWARE_RATELIMIT` (default OFF) — swaps the limiter `key_func` (and the shared `audit_client_ip` used at the 5 auth audit sites) from `get_remote_address` (the Railway edge-proxy TCP peer) to an X-Forwarded-For-derived client IP. **Effect ON:** distinct clients get distinct limiter keys / audit IPs. **HARD PRECONDITION before activation:** the current reader takes the *leftmost* XFF entry, which on Railway is attacker-controlled (the trusted edge appends the real peer to the RIGHT of any client-supplied XFF), so ON-as-written lets an attacker forge limiter keys and `admin_audit_log.ip_address`. The correct anchor depends on Railway's real proxy topology (hop count) and CANNOT be verified without live egress — prefer running uvicorn with `--proxy-headers --forwarded-allow-ips=<trusted edge>` (or read the hop the trusted edge appended, i.e. rightmost-after-trusted). Do NOT flip until this is verified against Railway's edge.
- `ENABLE_ANON_USAGE_GATE` (default OFF) — extends the freemium daily+monthly gate to ANONYMOUS callers on `/text/quick` and `/image/identify`, keyed on the regex-validated `X-Device-Fingerprint` header (`anon:{fp}` Redis counters, fail-open). **Effect ON:** an anonymous device over its free daily/monthly cap gets 429 `USAGE_LIMIT`. **Note:** an absent/garbage fingerprint no-ops the gate (metered clients only); the `@limiter.limit("10/minute")` decorator remains the backstop.

**Wave M13-W2 flags (currency truth — stop shipping a wrong number labelled genuine, branch `feature/m15-wave2-closeout` from `5ee72e8`, 2026-09-01).** M13 review Wave 2 landed findings `M13-07, M13-08, M13-09, M13-10, M13-11, M13-12, M13-38, M13-39, M13-40, M13-44`. **Flag-OFF byte-identity gate PROVEN:** `scripts/verify_flag_byte_identity.py` over `_proof` (414 pages / 1,656 calls) prints the SAME `OVERALL SHA256 cfd13914b43c12a623f0165a0367703b6cd52f6de75c557a3a725cf5db813de7` at `5ee72e8` and at the closeout HEAD with all five wave flags forced OFF. Every new flag is read PER CALL via `os.getenv` (never at import) and default OFF. Four NEW flags:
- `ENABLE_SHOPPING_STRICT_CURRENCY` (default OFF, M13-09) — the strict-label pend on the Serper-shopping tier (`extract_price_from_shopping`), the one price tier the BLOCKER-4 currency wave never covered. **Effect ON:** a candidate whose visible price carries a NON-target currency `detect_currency` cannot resolve PENDS instead of stamping the raw foreign amount with the target currency (the `1,399 د.إ` → 1399 BHD 9.8x-over-genuine bug), plus the `'$' in 'R$'` collision (BRL read as USD) pends. **Dispatcher over-rejection fix applied this closeout (`_GCC_SYMBOL_RESIDUE`):** a TARGET-currency price in its own Arabic glyph (`250 ر.س` on a SAR ask, `1,399 د.إ` on an AED ask) is GENUINE and must ship, not pend — the residue is resolved against a separator-stripped GCC glyph mirror BEFORE the non-ASCII catch-all, so target glyphs ship (flag ON == flag OFF) while foreign glyphs still pend. **Precondition:** flip WITH/AFTER `ENABLE_EXTENDED_FALLBACK_RATES` — the ASCII-ISO foreign residue (`TRY`/`PLN`/`CAD`/`JOD`…) only resolves to a pend once the extended table is on; with M13-09 ON and M13-38 OFF a `TRY 5050` shopping row still ships mislabelled.
- `ENABLE_REGION_CURRENCY_GUARD` (default OFF, M13-11) — the pend for a price whose currency ≠ the request region's currency (region currency via `exchange_rate_service.get_region_currency`). Applied at **TWO** places since M18 CD-interactions-02: a PRE-SCORING in-place pass `price_service.apply_region_currency_guard(product_data, region)` invoked by BOTH orchestrator paths (`structured_comparison_service`, immediately after `reconcile_pair_fairness` — sync before `compute_scores`, streaming before the `specs`/`prices` yields and `compute_scores`), plus the original `response_builder` price-pending chokepoint, kept as the idempotent backstop for direct `build_comparison_response` callers (share / history rebuilds, tests). **Effect ON:** a BHD price served to a `saudi_arabia` request pends (`amount None`, `unavailable True`, `guard_rejected=region_currency_mismatch` in metadata) — and, because the pend now lands BEFORE scoring, `winner_index` / `dimension_winners` / the LLM verdict take the honest missing-data path instead of being decided by the mismatched amount, and the SSE `prices` event carries the pending shape instead of flashing a foreign-currency amount the final `complete` then retracts. A same-region price, an already-pending price (`size_mismatch` / `unit_mismatch`) and a price with no currency label all pass untouched. The pre-scoring pass stashes its reason on the PRODUCT dict as `_region_guard_rejected` (never on the price — `public_price_view` only strips `_`-keys when `ENABLE_EXACT_PRICE_GATE` is ON) and `response_builder` harvests it before its `unavailable` early-continue, so `metadata.guard_rejected` survives the upstream pend. **The highest-blast-radius change in the wave — canary it LAST, alone.** **Precondition:** flip WITH/AFTER `ENABLE_LONGEST_HOST_MATCH` — with the host-match OFF, `ksa`/`om.swissarabian.com` mislabel their own SAR/OMR as USD, so the guard would wrongly pend those correct storefront prices in a Saudi/Oman region.
- `ENABLE_LONGEST_HOST_MATCH` (default OFF, M13-12) — one shared `_registry_row_for_host(host, where=, registry=)` longest-domain-wins resolver replacing every per-call `SOURCE_REGISTRY` suffix scan (`source_router.match_registry_apex`/`registry_tier`, `shopify_pdp_service`, `search_descriptor_service`). **Effect ON:** a currency/descriptor lookup resolves to the row with the LONGEST matching domain (exact match outright), fixing the 2-of-319 registry currency self-mismatches (`ksa.swissarabian.com` SAR, `om.swissarabian.com` OMR — both stamped USD OFF) and the 2-of-95 descriptor rows. Flag OFF = first-suffix-match, byte-identical.
- `ENABLE_EXTENDED_FALLBACK_RATES` (default OFF, M13-38) — widens `FALLBACK_RATES` with 10 corpus currencies (`TRY/PLN/CAD/JOD/SEK/DKK/CHF/EGP/NOK/AUD`) behind `effective_fallback_rates()`; `_convert_to_bhd` + `_normalize_currency_code` read the effective table. **Effect ON:** a `TRY 5050` price converts (≈47.47 BHD) instead of pending the dead rung. **Why dark:** the `_proof` corpus carries TRY/PLN/CAD/JOD pages that pend today, so widening the base dict unflagged would break flag-OFF byte-identity. Rates are approximate 2026 figures (the exact number is not load-bearing). Did NOT flip `ENABLE_RSC_FLIGHT_PRICE`.

`ENABLE_JSONLD_FIRST` (Wave-1, **default ON**) is REUSED — per the Wave-2 spec — for three currency changes, so **M13-07/M13-08/M13-40 are NOT dark: they ship LIVE the moment this deploys** (the byte-identity gate forces the flag OFF and does NOT exercise their production path). Independently corpus-verified at the prod default (JSONLD_FIRST ON, the four new flags OFF, base-vs-HEAD over the 414-page corpus): 0 LOST captures, ~94 NEW foreign-currency JSON-LD captures, provenance-only relabels at the same amount, and only 3 amount changes — all M13-40 preferring the authoritative JSON-LD Offer over a deviant OG fallback (e.g. `alhajisoman` OG 3.0 OMR anomaly → JSON-LD 30.0 OMR = 2.93→29.31 BHD, a 10x correction; `numberc` OG 22 KWD WAS-price → JSON-LD 12.10 KWD). Zero corpus regressions, but **watch the WooCommerce/foreign-offer capture on the first deploy, not at a later flag flip.** M13-07 = WooCommerce `converted_usd` relabel; M13-08 = parse the WooCommerce amount under the resolved `_wc_label` (de-DE `1.234` EUR → 505.94 BHD, NOT 0.51); M13-40 = a third `extract_price_from_html` pass accepting a foreign-currency JSON-LD Offer (45.00 EUR → 18.45 BHD `converted_usd`).

**Deliberately UNFLAGGED** (pure defect fixes, no behavioural fork, flag-OFF-equivalent for legitimate traffic): M13-10 (the tier1_shopping re-selection stash now parses `parse_price_string(price_str, detect_currency(price_str), display_text=True)` like the main path — `BHD 12,500` → 12.5 on both, not 12500.0); M13-44 (coerce a null `original_currency` to `''` at the three `.upper()` sites + `sanitize_gpt_price` — no `AttributeError`, degrade to Tier 3); **M13-39** (`_fallback_rate`/`get_rate` return `None` for an unknown currency instead of a silent rate of `1.0`; `get_rate` is now `Optional[float]`). **M13-39 decision (justified):** did NOT delete the `get_rate`/`_fetch_rates`/`_lookup_rate`/`_fallback_rate` cluster — two live test files import it (deletion = NEW import failures) and it has ZERO production callers (`_convert_to_bhd` uses `effective_fallback_rates()` directly, never `get_rate`), so the `1.0`→`None` change is confined to a dead function pair and cannot reach the extract path (confirmed by the identical flag-OFF gate SHA); 4 stale tests updated to the `None` contract.

**ACTIVATION coupling (Wave-2):** the four currency flags are canaried TOGETHER as a family, but with two hard orderings — (a) `ENABLE_EXTENDED_FALLBACK_RATES` with/before `ENABLE_SHOPPING_STRICT_CURRENCY` (else TRY/PLN/CAD ASCII-ISO shopping rows still ship mislabelled), (b) `ENABLE_LONGEST_HOST_MATCH` with/before `ENABLE_REGION_CURRENCY_GUARD` (else the guard pends correct swissarabian SAR/OMR prices), (c) **the M18 CD-interactions-02 pre-scoring pass must be MERGED before the region-guard flip** — before it, the guard pended only the FINAL projection, so a final-payload-only canary reads CLEAN (the payload shows `price: pending`) while the winner, the dimension winners and the LLM verdict are still decided by the mismatched amount and the SSE `prices` event still flashes it mid-stream. A canary for this flag must therefore inspect the `scores` event / `winner_index` and the mid-stream `prices` event, not just the `complete` payload — and **`ENABLE_REGION_CURRENCY_GUARD` goes LAST, in its own window** (highest blast radius: in any non-Bahrain region it pends every non-region-currency price). Remember M13-07/08/40 are already live on deploy via default-ON `ENABLE_JSONLD_FIRST`; the only kill-switch for them is disabling the whole Wave-1 JSON-LD reorder.

**ACTIVATION-ORDER runbook (when validation begins — one canary compare per step, never batch across steps):** (1) `ENABLE_EXTRACTOR_FIXES_2608` + the three M5 price adapters (`ENABLE_RSC_FLIGHT_PRICE`, `ENABLE_MAGENTO_GQL_ADAPTER`, `ENABLE_SALLA_SLUG_RESOLVE`) together — all additive rungs on no-price residuals. (2) `ENABLE_YOTPO_REVIEWS` + `ENABLE_JUDGEME_HTML_REVIEWS` — enrichment only; watch latency, not correctness. **CAVEAT 2026-09-06 (code-review CR-COMPLEXITY-DEADCODE-04): both flags gate modules with ZERO production importers (`yotpo_service.py`, `judgeme_service.py`), so this step can only no-op until the adapter is wired into `review_service` (Yotpo) — judge.me is forbidden by the licensing ruling and is on the delete list.** (3) The sitemap trio together with `ENABLE_SITEMAP_INDEX`: flip `ENABLE_SITEMAP_PDP_MARKERS_V2` → **rebuild the index** → then `ENABLE_SITEMAP_MATCH_V2`; never `MATCH_V2` against a stale index. (4) `ENABLE_FRAGRANCE_GLASS_EXEMPTION` alone (fragrance selection outcome changes). (5) `ENABLE_NORMALIZE_WORDS_EMPTY_FIX` **alone in its own window** — global matcher blast radius; nothing else may move with it. (6) Seed first, then flip `ENABLE_SPEC_SPINE` and `ENABLE_SEARCH_DESCRIPTOR`. (7) `ENABLE_SPECS_NO_FABRICATION` **LAST**, only after search restoration + D1. **M9/M10 insertions:** `ENABLE_NOT_A_PDP_FILTER` + `ENABLE_WALL_SIGNATURE_ANCHOR` + `ENABLE_UCP_JSON_PRICE` join step (1)'s additive-capture family; `ENABLE_VISIBLE_TEXT_CURRENCY` gets its OWN window between steps (4) and (5) — it is a currency-LABEL change and must never move with another flag.

**M20 winner-truth completion (issues `#99` + `#110`, on top of the concurrent PRs #122/#124, 2026-09-01).** PR #122 shipped `#99` PARTIALLY and that half-fix was LIVE: `_names_the_loser()` gated the loser-prose drop on case-insensitive containment of the loser's **FULL name**, and only that. **MEASURED against the shipped `build_comparison_response` on a mismatch row: 4 of 5 realistic phrasings shipped the loser's praise verbatim** into `overview.winner.reason` -> the top-level `recommendation`, Home `verdict_short`, History and the **Share text** (pronoun "It offers a noticeably richer flavour"; short name "Budget Pickle wins on everyday value." — which still NAMES the loser beside the winner's name; brand fragment; subject-free praise). It is STRUCTURAL, not unlucky: `extraction_service` caps `winner_reason` at 20 words (`:953`, `:983`) while a full product name runs 5-6, so the prompt pushes the model AWAY from the one string the check looked for — and because the declaration is also dropped, a dangling "It" re-points at the SHIPPED winner, silently converting praise-of-the-loser into fabricated praise-of-the-winner. **Fix: drop `winner_reason` AND `key_tradeoff` UNCONDITIONALLY whenever the deterministic winner overrode GPT's** — on a mismatch that prose exists to justify the product we did NOT pick, so containment was the wrong question entirely; `_names_the_loser` is deleted. Re-measured after the fix: **0 leaking fields across all 5 phrasings**, and the AGREEMENT path keeps GPT's prose byte-identical (pinned both ways, plus a parametrized 5-phrasing pin so this cannot regress to a containment check). Also landed: a non-int `winner_index` normalization (`comparison` is raw model JSON — `ComparisonResult` is never applied to it, so a JSON `null` or quoted `'0'` read as a genuine disagreement and destroyed prose that already named the right product).
- `ENABLE_WINNER_PROSE_RECONCILE` (default OFF, `#110`) — the UNFLAGGED safety layer above makes name/index truthful everywhere the payload exposes a winner (`overview.winner.name`, the BC `comparison.winner_index`, and the **SSE `verdict` event's `product_index` + name**, which previously flipped mid-stream against `complete`). This flag adds the optional layer on top: it REPLACES the dropped strings with a deterministic template instead of leaving the qualitative fallback. **Effect ON:** user-facing verdict COPY changes on the core surface (and on Home `verdict_short`, a second copy surface) — so it is dark and canaried ALONE. Flag OFF = the safety layer only.

**Wave M13-W3 flags (event-loop & deadline integrity, branch `feature/m16-wave3-closeout` from `e22258e`, 2026-09-01).** M13 review Wave 3 landed findings `M13-04, M13-05, M13-06, M13-30, M13-31, M13-32, M13-33, M13-34, M13-35, M13-37`. **Flag-OFF byte-identity gate PROVEN:** `scripts/verify_flag_byte_identity.py` over `_proof` (414 pages / 1,656 calls) prints the SAME `OVERALL SHA256 298f2ced4dd4bc313ae871b97ba7d64bedfe16d94dea182a9c7b53c358b87630` at `e22258e` and at the closeout HEAD with all four wave flags forced OFF (`ENABLE_SYNC_DB_OFFLOAD`, `ENABLE_SERPER_BREAKER`, `ENABLE_FULL_STREAM_DEADLINE`, `ENABLE_ASYNC_REDIS_OFFLOAD`). The extraction path (`price_service`/`platform_router`/`shopify_pdp_service`/`exchange_rate_service`/`source_router`/`scoring_service`) is byte-identically UNTOUCHED (empty diff), so the corpus SHA cannot move. Every new flag is read PER CALL via `os.getenv` (never at import) and default OFF. Four dark offloads:
- `ENABLE_SYNC_DB_OFFLOAD` (default OFF, M13-05) - `app/utils/db_offload.run_db(call)` moves the REQUEST-PATH HOT SET of blocking Supabase `.execute()` off the loop via `asyncio.to_thread`. **Effect ON:** one user's blocking DB RTT no longer stalls every other coroutine on single-worker uvicorn. **Hot set (9 sites):** `database_service.{get_user_by_id,get_user_comparisons,save_comparison}`, `usage_service.{_get_user_tier_info,_get_active_referral_bonus,record_comparison(increment rpc),record_lifetime_comparison(rpc)}`, `auth_service.verify_token`, `audit_service.log_audit_event`. **Flag OFF:** `run_db` is an `async def` with no `await` on the inline branch -> byte-identical to a direct `.execute()`. **Precondition:** none; canary alone. **Deferred list CLOSED 2026-09-02 by M18 `#115`** - every site named here (`usage_service._maybe_reset_referral_bonus`, `_persist_genuine_price` set_cached, `database_service.log_search`, `feedback_service.save_comparison_and_track_cohort`) is now wrapped, plus the product_data L2 executes, the auth/behaviour-profile reads and the referral Loop-2 entry queries. Still deliberately left: off-path/cron/script sync-Supabase sites (off-clock by design) and the behaviour-profile RPC (needs migration 037). `usage_service.get_usage_status`/`check_usage_allowed` (whose two Supabase reads already go through `run_db` via `_get_user_tier_info` / `_get_active_referral_bonus`) and the `review_service` L1 read + post-extraction writes are covered since 2026-10-23 by their own dark flag `ENABLE_USAGE_PATH_OFFLOOP` (ST1: the two usage counter GETs and those reviews L1 calls move to the shared `redis-offloop` pool; see the SESSION 81 row).
- `ENABLE_ASYNC_REDIS_OFFLOAD` (**pre-existing, default UNCHANGED OFF**, EXTENDED by M13-06) — now also mirrors 10 request-path `set_cached` WRITES (`_cache_set_async`) and batches the firecrawl+scrapedo render-gate reads (`is_circuit_closed`+`has_budget`) via `_provider_gate_ok_async` (deliberately NOT memoised — a breaker trip must short-circuit the next render candidate). **Flag OFF:** both inline, byte-identical. **⛔ HARD PRECONDITION before any canary of THIS flag:** `is_circuit_closed`'s half-open counter (`api_budget_service.py`, M13-31) is a NON-atomic Redis GET→incr→SET. Flag OFF it is safe (inline-sync, no loop yield, so concurrent render-gate checks serialise). Flag ON wraps it in `asyncio.to_thread`, so concurrent fan-out threads run the RMW in parallel, several read `half_open_calls=0`, and MULTIPLE render probes are admitted in the breaker's half-open window (paid render spend leak). RESOLVE FIRST: make `half_open_calls` an atomic Redis INCR (M13-37 already uses INCRBY for exactly this counter class — mirror it) OR exclude the provider render gate from the offload.
- `ENABLE_PRICE_PATH_REDIS_OFFLOOP` (default OFF, S77 REDIS-OFFLOOP, read PER CALL) + knob `REDIS_OFFLOOP_MAX_WORKERS` (default 16, clamped 1..32, unparsable or non-finite -> 16, read ONCE when the pool is built, so a change needs a restart; not set on `web`, a value above 20 needs its own measured window). **Problem (measured 2026-10-11):** the cached Shopify catalogs (`shopify_catalog:<domain>`, 0.3-2.4 MB each, a GET of the largest 0.9-2.2 s) were read synchronously on the event loop by `price_service._fetch_shopify_catalog`, 18-36 times per cold compare, with no flag covering them; one cold compare froze the loop 6.5-16.2 s. **Effect ON:** `app/utils/redis_offloop.run_redis_offloop(fn, *args, durable=...)` (a stdlib-only leaf; the callable comes from the caller's own namespace, so a test patching `price_service.get_cached` intercepts both branches) runs each call as ONE job on a DEDICATED `redis-offloop` `ThreadPoolExecutor`, in a copied contextvars context: the Shopify catalog GET plus its negcache and catalog SETs, the bolo/boutiqaat `resolve_pdp_via_sitemap` lookup (the whole sync function: meta + bucket GETs + slug match), the Algolia harvest-config GET/SETs, the `nogenuine:` sentinel read (+ `sitemap_domains_now_built`) in `_get_price`, and the reviews L1 read. Writes are `asyncio.shield`ed (`durable=True`), so a dispatched SET lands even when the caller is cancelled, as it does inline; reads queued behind a full pool are dropped on cancel. **Kept INLINE under ON (RO16/RO17, fix round 1):** both `_record_negative_price_cache` terminals of `_get_price` (S4b) and the reviews L2-promotion write stay the exact base inline calls: as a durable FIFO pool job the `nogenuine:` sentinel SET could land AFTER a concurrent request's #53 sentinel DELETE (reproduced 47/50 with the real methods and `ENABLE_NEGCACHE_GENUINE_INVALIDATION` on), leaving a stale 30-day estimate sentinel beside a genuine L1 price, while at base the section is loop-atomic; the promotion write is the same reorder class. Moving either later needs an ordered write path (delete-after-set fencing or a per-key sequence). **Why not the shared `qaren-worker` pool:** with `ENABLE_UPSTASH_BOUNDED_TRANSPORT` OFF the SDK's `httpx.Client(timeout=None)` can park a thread with no ceiling; the dedicated pool bounds that to 16 threads, never touches the adapter fan-out's 96, and stays under the SDK client's 20 keepalive connections. **Flag OFF:** the helper's branch has no `await`: the same calls, order, args, keys, values and TTLs, on the loop thread, byte-identical, and no pool is built. **Composes with `ENABLE_ASYNC_REDIS_OFFLOAD`:** disjoint call sites, separate executors, no double dispatch. **UNFLAGGED alongside it (RO2, logging only, response bytes unchanged):** the silent `TimeoutError` branches of the collective bahrain Shopify consume (3.0 s cap) and Algolia consume (`_ALGOLIA_TIER2_TIMEOUT` 5.0 s) now log `[ADAPTER DROP] shopify: CONSUME-BOUND after <cap:.2f>s` / `[ADAPTER DROP] algolia: CONSUME-BOUND after <cap:.2f>s` (the cap recorded with the R-W18 walrus). **Stated limits:** (1) left on the loop: the S4b negcache terminals and the reviews L2-promotion write (RO16/RO17 above), the adapter breaker gates/recorders (Algolia/noon/unbxd/nasser; a breaker RMW review is owed), the Serper exhausted-key read (inert with one key), the `review_service` post-extraction writes (the F3 ordering; moved off the loop under `ENABLE_USAGE_PATH_OFFLOOP`, ST1, 2026-10-23), `_match_shopify_product` and fresh-catalog `resp.json()` CPU, the GIL-held `json.loads`/base64 decode of a multi-MB payload (about 10-20 ms each), the duplicate per-product GET of the same catalog (no memo); (2) the MAIN capture risk ON is the collective all-or-nothing consume caps (Shopify 3.0 s, Algolia 5.0 s), alongside the per-source 10 s `_timeout_none`: a catalog GET waiting in the 16-wide pool can now let a cap expire where OFF finished while freezing everything (the CONSUME-BOUND lines are the counter); (3) ONE FIFO pool: small reads (negcache, reviews L1, sitemap meta, Algolia config) queue behind catalog GETs within and across concurrent compares, so under concurrent load the 15 s price race / 10 s reviews `wait_for` can expire on queue wait (follow-up: a small-call lane or a per-process single-flight memo for `shopify_catalog:<domain>`); (4) memory: up to 16 concurrent catalog decodes at about 3x JSON size live (synthetic measurement only); (5) interpreter exit drains QUEUED pool items as well as running ones (non-daemon threads); (6) the awaited durable writes (the catalog SET on a miss, the Shopify and Algolia neg SETs, the Algolia config SET) sit on the caller's critical path, so a deadline can fire during them and the write still lands (RO5c); (7) the SDK's shared header dict: a once-per-process first-command race degrades to one fail-open miss; (8) measured by the adversary (real helper, 16 workers, synthetic GET timings): FIFO waits of 0.5-3.4 s for the small reads (negcache, reviews L1) under 1-3 concurrent cold fragrance compares, a pool capacity of about 0.77 cold fragrance compares/s, still strictly better than the ~63 s of serial loop block OFF for 3 compares (108 GETs); (9) the S1b neg-sentinel SET can reorder against a concurrent catalog SET of the same `shopify_catalog:<domain>` key (rare, self-consistent, bounded by the 30-min neg TTL); (10) this pool and `ENABLE_ASYNC_REDIS_OFFLOAD`'s `qaren-worker` calls share the ONE SDK `httpx.Client` (max_connections=100, 20 keepalive), so with both flags ON their concurrent Upstash calls add up against that one connection pool; (11) a durable write whose event loop closes before it completes logs an ERROR from its done-callback in short-lived scripts (never on web, whose loop lives for the process); (12) the executor submit raises RuntimeError if no pool thread can be started (thread exhaustion; negligible). **/health (RO18):** `redis_offloop_pool` {`workers`, `queued`} from `redis_offloop_pool_stats()` appears only once the pool exists (`ENABLE_PRICE_PATH_REDIS_OFFLOOP` or, since ST1 (2026-10-23), `ENABLE_USAGE_PATH_OFFLOOP` was ON at least once in this process: both flags dispatch to this one pool); with no pool the body is byte-identical to base (no key, no null); a non-dict or raising stats call leaves the key out (`app.main.redis_offloop_pool_snapshot`, the `price_parse_pool` shape). **Canary (RO1-RO3, RO8):** merge with the flag unset (no behaviour change); `b_pre` = a pre-flip canary on the new deploy with the flag OFF (state recorded as `ENABLE_OFFLOOP_DNS_RESOLVE=true`, `ENABLE_PRICE_PARSE_OFFLOAD=false`); the flip needs the owner's explicit yes; `c1` uses 3 NEVER-compared pairs category-matched to `b_pre`'s (at least 2 in fragrances / makeup / other), written into the ledger before running, never a rerun of `b_pre`'s pairs. Watch `/health loop_lag_max_60s_ms` (b0: 16190 ms), `/health redis_offloop_pool.queued` (a value that persists between compares is a stall signal, RO20), /health misses, READY count, the shopify/algolia CONSUME-BOUND count and per-pair prices (c1 must not be worse than `b_pre`), the `Redis GET error` / `Cache set error` / `Future exception was never retrieved` rate, `[REDIS-OFFLOOP] pool built workers=16` exactly once per process, the Railway memory graph and drain time. **Rollback:** unset the variable; the next call is inline and the pool idles.
- `ENABLE_SERPER_BREAKER` (default OFF, M13-32) — breaker-gated wrapper over renamed `_serper_post_impl`. **Effect ON:** an OPEN `serper` breaker short-circuits the POST (returns None → benign-empty, same shape as budget-out), records failure on timeout/5xx/403 and success on 200; breaker read memoised on `SERPER_GATE_CACHE_TTL`, invalidated on each record so a trip engages next call. A benign budget-out/no-key None-return records NOTHING (`if response is not None` guard) so it never trips on a wrong signal. **Flag OFF:** `_serper_post` delegates directly, byte-identical. **Precondition:** none.
- `ENABLE_FULL_STREAM_DEADLINE` (default OFF, M13-04) — bounds the post-Phase-1 streaming verdict tail. **Effect ON:** deadline at generator entry; verdict + self-critique + moderation each run under a residual-budget `asyncio.wait_for` (residual floors at 0.05s), yielding the best-available PARTIAL on expiry. **Flag OFF:** each awaits directly (today's unbounded tail — byte-identical). **Precondition:** none, but pairing it ON with the streaming route bounds the M13-35 early-disconnect amplification below.

**SESSION 78 flag (S78-SM, 2026-10-14; default OFF, read PER CALL; a new name, so unset in production by construction (Railway not read); plan fix 5 of `docs/investigations/2026-10-14-session-78-state/COMPARE_QUALITY_PLAN_S78.md`, rulings SM1-SM6 + SM-ADV-1..9 + SM-G1..G3 in that folder's `specs/`):**
- `ENABLE_SERPER_KEY_STATE_MEMO` + knob `SERPER_KEY_STATE_MEMO_TTL` (S78-SM, `app/services/serper_service.py::_serper_key_state_memo_enabled`; read path `_is_serper_key_exhausted` -> `_is_serper_key_exhausted_memoised`; invalidation `_invalidate_serper_key_state`, called by `_mark_serper_key_exhausted`; the knob is read per store by `_serper_key_state_memo_ttl()`, default 60.0 s, unset / blank / garbage / inf / nan / <= 0 -> 60.0). **Problem (measured at base `e0f3e475`):** in multi-key mode (`SERPER_API_KEYS` with 2+ keys) `_active_serper_key` reads the Redis flag `serper:exhausted:<prefix8>` synchronously ON the event loop at every search entry guard and every rotation-loop iteration: 2 GETs per Serper call with no key flagged, 4 with the first key flagged, plus `api_budget_service._serper_key_prefix`'s own 1-2 per Serper 200 (6 in all per call with the first key flagged, at 20-28 Serper calls per compare). Single-key mode reads nothing. **Effect ON:** the answer (True AND False) is memoised per process per Redis flag key (keys sharing an 8-char prefix share one entry, as they share one flag) for the TTL: one GET per flag key per window, the clock read BEFORE the GET, expiry at `now >= expires_at`, the TTL read at each store; `_mark_serper_key_exhausted` invalidates the entry AFTER its Redis SET, in both flag states (a dict pop + a generation bump, no I/O), so an in-process mark is honoured on the next call (one re-read GET; with the SET failed the re-read is the base fail-open False); a per-flag-key generation drops a GET answer that raced a mark; one `threading.Lock` guards the two dicts and is never held across the GET; a raising helper (incl. a non-str key) returns fail-open False and is NOT memoised. Steady state per Serper call: 3 -> 1 GET (no key flagged), 6 -> 2 (first key flagged). No new log line. **Flag OFF:** byte-identical on the read path: the base body runs unchanged and the memo is neither read nor written there; the only OFF-state memo write is the mark's unconditional invalidate (a dict pop + a generation bump, no Redis call, no log; pinned by p5d; best-effort, so a non-str key cannot raise there, SM-COR-1); pinned by the committed 30-record golden `tests/fixtures/s78_sm_flag_off_redis_sequence.json` (5 key modes x 6 entry points x 3 consecutive calls: the full ordered Redis call list with arguments and TTLs and every POSTed key) under unset / `false` / `0` / `off` / `""` (`tests/test_s78_serper_key_memo.py`). `tests/conftest.py` resets the memo before every test (`_reset_serper_key_state_memo()`, beside the budget-memo reset), so a developer `.env` carrying the flag cannot leak a memo across tests. **Stated limits:** (L1) `api_budget_service._serper_key_prefix` keeps its own per-call GET (1-2 per Serper 200), so ON removes about two thirds of the flag reads, not all (follow-up S78-SM-b: the same memo there, closing L1 and L9; plan fix 4, dropping the exhausted key, removes both); (L2) a mark made by another process (`price-warmer`, a second replica) is seen only when the window expires: at most one wasted depletion POST per key per process, which then marks locally and rotates; (L3) a re-funded key whose Redis flag is deleted stays skipped for up to the TTL in each process, and if every listed key is memoised exhausted, every Serper leg returns empty for up to the TTL per process after the flags are cleared (OFF recovers on the next call; a canary watch item); (L4) `_redis_get` swallows errors, so during a Redis error a fail-open False (the base value) is memoised for the window, and after Redis recovers inside that window it can be stale, bounded to one wasted depletion POST per key because the mark invalidates the entry (`Redis GET error` lines fall to 1 per key per window); (L6) concurrent cold readers on worker threads each GET (no single flight; event-loop callers never interleave); (L9) a memoised True can outlive a cleared Redis flag by up to the TTL, which misattributes the lifetime counter (`record_usage` scopes by its own read); response bytes are unaffected; the gain on Railway is unmeasured (an Upstash GET was 163-203 ms with TLS in #60 / S77). **Precondition:** none to flip, but the flag is INERT unless `SERPER_API_KEYS` lists 2+ keys. **Canary (the owner's explicit yes, fresh pairs):** `/health loop_lag_max_60s_ms`, the Upstash daily command count, Serper leg timings and the `SERPER_KEY_EXHAUSTED` WARNING lines. **Rollback:** unset the variable (per call, immediate; the memo then idles).

**M18 CD-interactions-01 flag (SSE pre-verdict disconnect, `app/api/text_routes.py`, 2026-09-01).** The W3 x W3 interaction above (M13-35 drain defeating the M13-37 refund) is fixed in two halves, shipped differently on purpose - shipping both unflagged would leave a canary unable to tell an accounting change from a latency/spend change, and shipping both dark would leave the live credit burn in place behind a flag nobody has flipped.
- *Half A - accounting, deliberately UNFLAGGED* (same category as M13-10/M13-44: a pure defect correction with no result fork for legitimate completed traffic). `event_generator` now latches `complete_after_client_gone` on the FIRST `complete`/`settle_complete` event and the metering branch reads `if complete_response and not had_error and not complete_after_client_gone`. **Effect:** a client that leaves BEFORE the final payload falls through to the existing `else` and refunds via `usage_refund.text_stream.incomplete` - no `log_search` success row, no `save_comparison`, no `record_lifetime_comparison`, net quota effect zero as it was pre-W3. A client that leaves AFTER the verdict/`settle_complete` still meters and persists exactly once (M13-35's case, pinned). `elif had_error` still wins when an `error` event was also seen, so exactly one refund fires, never two. `usage_consumed is False` and anonymous callers refund on no path.
- `ENABLE_PREVERDICT_DISCONNECT_ABORT` (**default OFF**) - *Half B, resource.* **Effect ON:** a PRE-verdict disconnect also stops driving the orchestrator - the bound generator is `aclose()`d and the loop breaks - so the verdict/critique/moderation OpenAI tail is not paid for a comparison nobody will read. The explicit `aclose()` is load-bearing: a bare `break` out of an `async for` does NOT close the generator, CPython finalizes it later and non-deterministically, so the orchestrator's `finally` (and M13-30's `_get_price` prefetch cancellation) would not run at the break. **Flag OFF:** the `break` never fires and nothing is ever closed - the route makes exactly the same calls on the generator as it did at `dd4c849`, so the M13-35 drain is unchanged. Read PER CALL via `os.getenv` (`text_routes.preverdict_disconnect_abort_enabled`). **Canary precondition: none**; pairing with `ENABLE_FULL_STREAM_DEADLINE` is complementary, not required. Deliberately out of scope: no `success=False` "abandoned" `log_search` row on the aborted path (it would move the KPI failure-rate series; pre-wave this path logged nothing) - raise separately if the analytics gap matters.

`ADAPTER_EXECUTOR_MAX_WORKERS` (env knob, NOT a feature flag, default 40, M13-34) sizes the named `qaren-worker` `ThreadPoolExecutor` installed as the loop default at startup (`main.py` on_event). UNFLAGGED, always-on: it replaces CPython's host-dependent default pool (`min(32, cpu+4)`) for every `to_thread`/`run_in_executor(None)` site. Benign — on realistic hosts the ceiling only rises or holds (prod ~32→40, small box ~6→40), so it can only keep/improve capture; the corpus gate is (correctly) insensitive to it.

**Six UNFLAGGED invariant-tightening fixes (land with a pin; no happy-path result fork):** M13-31 (count the half-open probe so exactly ONE is admitted — fires only on the breaker OPEN→HALF_OPEN degraded path; CLOSED/no-state returns before it), M13-33 (`review_service` L2 persist → `fire_and_forget` so an RLS/schema/5xx failure is logged, not swallowed — adds logging only), M13-30 (`_get_price` body wrapped in try/finally so an outer cancel cancels the ~18 prefetch tasks — pure whitespace re-indent, `git diff -w` shows only try/finally/comment, idempotent cleanup), M13-34 (one adapter inner-timeout clamp + sized executor — see the two deviations below), M13-35 (SSE post-verdict metering moved into a `finally` + drain-not-abandon on disconnect so a client dropping after the verdict still meters/persists), M13-37 (atomic freemium consume at the gate closing the quota TOCTOU). **M13-37 closeout fix (dispatcher-applied, `feature/m16-wave3-closeout`):** the failure-path refund now fires BEFORE `_surface_comparison_failure` in POST+GET — that call RAISES for TIMEOUT/INSUFFICIENT_DATA/generic, so a refund after it was unreachable dead code that burned a daily credit on every cold-path timeout — plus a symmetric `else`-refund in the streaming `finally`. **CORRECTED 2026-09-01 (M18 CD-interactions-01):** that `else`-refund covers a mid-stream raise and a cancel (the `finally` runs on CancelledError/GeneratorExit too) but it did NOT cover a real pre-verdict client disconnect, which is what the original note claimed. M13-35's drain-not-abandon means a disconnect at second 2 still runs the generator to completion, so `complete_response` ends up set, the `finally` takes the METERING branch, and the `else` (which requires `complete_response` absent) is unreachable - a network blip burned one of the 3 daily / 10 monthly / 3 lifetime free comparisons for a result the user never received, where pre-wave a dropped connection was free. Fixed unflagged by the `complete_after_client_gone` latch in `text_routes.event_generator` (see the M18 entry under **Feature Flags**); a POST-verdict drop still meters, which is M13-35's deliberate behaviour and is pinned.

**Two UNFLAGGED behavioral deviations M13-34 introduces (byte-identity gate cannot see timing; canary-watch):** (a) adapter inner timeout clamped 10s→9s for woo/salla/occ/magento/rest_json — a source responding in the 9.0–10.0s window now returns None where base returned a price (defensible parked-thread prevention; the 15s race + other tiers backstop; `ENABLE_BH_GCC_CATALOG_SOURCES` ON can fire these on the live path). (b) Bright Data inner SERP timeout clamped 20s→9s — a fallback result arriving in the 9–15s window (previously deliverable inside the 15s `_PRICE_RACE_TIMEOUT`) is now cut at 9s (rarely-firing paid Serper-depletion fallback; untestable without paid spend). Both are canary-watch items, NOT the "no result change" the implementer note claimed.

**M13-35 resource note:** drain-not-abandon runs the full Phase-2 OpenAI tail server-side even when the client disconnects BEFORE the verdict, and that tail is default-UNBOUNDED (`ENABLE_FULL_STREAM_DEADLINE` default OFF). An early connect-then-drop now amplifies into a full server-side compare where it previously self-cancelled. **CORRECTED 2026-09-01 (M18 CD-interactions-01) - this was NOT a pure resource trade-off.** The same drain also silently BURNED A USER CREDIT on every pre-verdict drop (it defeated the M13-37 refund, see the correction at the M13-37 bullet above), so the "if the amplification matters" framing understated it. The accounting half is now fixed UNFLAGGED; the resource half - stopping the drain so the default-unbounded OpenAI tail is not paid for - ships dark behind `ENABLE_PREVERDICT_DISCONNECT_ABORT` (default OFF), so **until that flag is flipped the amplification described here is still live**. `ENABLE_FULL_STREAM_DEADLINE` remains complementary, not required.

Operational rollout sequence + canary monitoring guidance: see `docs/CONTEXT_SESSION_LOG.md`. M11 closeout references: robots-unreadable policy ruling `docs/policies/2026-08-31-robots-unreadable-ruling.md`; Ahmed's unblock pack (OpenAI canary GO, Firecrawl top-up + Redis latch, affiliate signups) `docs/runbooks/2026-08-31-ahmed-unblock-pack.md`.

**M20/M21 flag additions not yet recorded above (all default OFF, all read PER CALL via `os.getenv` so a Railway flip needs no restart; flag-OFF byte-identical, test-pinned per flag):**
- `ENABLE_CATEGORY_VALUE_BADGE` (M20 W1, #102) — `value_badge` resolves the category's value dimension from `_DIMENSION_SIGNAL_MAP` instead of a hardcoded key only 2 of 9 categories have (OFF = the constant `fair_price` for the other 7).
- `ENABLE_BEHAVIORAL_DIM_TRANSLATION` (M20 W1, #103) — re-keys the LEGACY universal sensitivity keys (`spec_score`/`review_score`/`price_score`) onto category dimensions at APPLICATION time (emission untouched — rewriting the emitter would strand stored profiles), and derives `scoring_method` from what actually MOVED the weights. Golden-pinned flag-OFF (`tests/fixtures/behavioral_flag_off_golden.json`).
- `ENABLE_FACTCHECK_CURRENCY_NORMALIZATION` (#106) — `verify_price` converts every shopping row into the final price's currency before taking the median (mirrors `extract_price_from_shopping` row handling). Bare-numeral rows parse under the TARGET currency (PR #125: BHD `99.500` = 99.5, not 99500 — BHD is minor-unit-3 and that is the ordinary Bahraini price format); rows with an unresolvable currency basis are dropped, and zero-usable-rows yields `price_verified: None`, never a fabricated verdict.
- `ENABLE_SPEC_CONFIDENCE_CACHE` (#107) — carries the derived `_spec_citation_confidence` map across the L1/L2 spec cache (never raw snippets), so citation verification is no longer dead on every cache hit.
- `ENABLE_CITATION_RUBRIC_V2` (#108) — unit-aware citation rubric with a reachable `flagged` outcome (separator-stripped both sides incl. Arabic U+066C; number+unit pairing; number-free snippets grade `unverified`, not `likely`).
- `ENABLE_COMPARISON_QUALITY_V2` (M21 W3, PO-verdict-text-09) — sparse spec coverage classifies `weak` instead of `weird` (`weird` was the modal label on real traffic, 14/15 recorded rows, fired by thin early-pipeline data), and the real data-quality signal is threaded into the verdict prompt so the model can hedge on thin evidence. FE renders weak-mode; check celebration gating before the OTA that follows a flip.
- `ENABLE_TPM_AWARE_ROUTING` (#117) — **PHANTOM at `76ace90`: zero code sites (verified 2026-09-06 review, code-review B.3); #117 shipped only `OPENAI_MAX_RETRIES`/`OPENAI_FALLBACK_MAX_RETRIES`. Kept here as the unbuilt plan:** per-minute token accounting for the model router (the daily 1M counter is TPM-blind; Tier-1 caps the restored product at ~6.6-10 compares/min). Knobs ride it; `OPENAI_MAX_RETRIES` (default 2 = the SDK default) is env-resolved separately and unflagged.
- M21 W4 (unflagged, PR #130): the 8 adapter convertibility gates consult one shared `exchange_rate_service.is_convertible()`, so `ENABLE_EXTENDED_FALLBACK_RATES` now reaches every adapter (a canary no longer under-measures on adapter-covered hosts). One deliberate flag-OFF change rode along: salla's hand-copied mirror predated SGD/JPY/CNY/INR, which the base table converts everywhere else — salla now matches the other adapters instead of silently dropping those stores.
- Hard precondition pinned in **#128**: `refund_anon_comparison_credit` has NO callers — wire it into the anon failure paths of `/text/quick` + `/image/identify` BEFORE canarying `ENABLE_ANON_USAGE_GATE`, or a failed anonymous comparison burns a credit that previously cost nothing.

**Wave W0 flags (SESSION 65 load foundations — the event-loop taxes no existing flag could remove; branch `feature/s65-w0-load-foundations`, 2026-09-07).** Every one defaults OFF and is flag-OFF byte-identical to `286f132`, proven by `scripts/verify_flag_byte_identity.py` over the cached `_proof` corpus (414 pages / 1,656 calls). **These land BEFORE any offload canary and before `#114`** — they are what makes the single uvicorn worker safe to load. Read the per-flag caveats: unlike the price-path flags, two of these are NOT live-flippable.
- `ENABLE_OFFLOOP_DNS_RESOLVE` (W0-1, **the P0** LS-REQUEST-PATH-BLOCKING-01, read PER CALL) — `app/utils/url_validator.py` ran a synchronous, untimeoutable libc `socket.getaddrinfo` from inside `async def` bodies: every unauthenticated `/api/v1/url/*` handler (`/detect` is 20/min, `/retailers` unlimited), the URL-extraction path and the price path called it once per URL and once per redirect hop, so ONE black-holed host froze the whole worker for the OS resolver timeout (11–12 s measured) before a byte reached the wire. **Effect ON:** the resolve runs in a DEDICATED `dns-resolve` `ThreadPoolExecutor` (`DNS_RESOLVER_POOL_SIZE`, default **16**, CLAMPED to 1..64 — read ONCE when the pool is first built, so changing it needs a restart; 16 because the ordinary price fan-out runs 10-20 CONCURRENT validations, so a pool of 4 made queue-induced timeouts the normal case, and the clamp stops an ops typo from defeating the bounded-zombie design) under `DNS_RESOLVE_TIMEOUT_SECONDS` (default 2.0, per call), failing CLOSED on both `TimeoutError` and `gaierror` exactly like today's `gaierror` branch, with a bounded 60 s memo (512 entries, keyed on hostname) over **CONFIRMED NEGATIVES ONLY**. The pool is deliberately NOT the shared `qaren-worker` executor: a libc `getaddrinfo` cannot be cancelled and `wait_for` cancels only the await, so the zombie threads must be bounded by this pool alone. 11 call sites await one helper (`url_routes` ×6, `url_extraction_service` :101/:111, `price_service.curl_fetch_html_same_site` :14016/:14037, `shopify_pdp_service._fetch_once` via a new `_hop_is_allowed_async`); flag OFF that helper's branch contains no `await`, so the sync validator runs inline with no new suspension point. **Two verdicts are deliberately NEVER memoised, both after measurement:** a SUCCESSFUL public resolve (memoising it turned the guard's millisecond-wide DNS-rebinding TOCTOU into a deterministic 60 s replay window on these UNAUTHENTICATED routes — a host that rebound to `169.254.169.254` kept validating True where base returns False), and a timeout whose resolve NEVER STARTED (a `ThreadPoolExecutor` queues past its workers while `wait_for`'s clock starts at the await, so a queued-only call would blacklist a host nothing looked up — measured at the old pool of 4, four black-holed hosts poisoned 8 of 8 unrelated healthy storefronts, making flag ON worse than OFF for capture). A `threading.Event` set by the resolver callable tells a genuinely slow host (memoised — the P0's win) from queue contention (fails closed for that call only). **Canary-watch:** a STARTED resolve slower than the 2 s bound is a 60 s block for that host, not a per-call retry; a public host is re-resolved on EVERY call, so the memo never shortens repeat positive lookups; pool threads are non-daemon, so a parked resolve can delay process shutdown by the OS resolver timeout — watch Railway drain time on the first flip.
- `ENABLE_SUPABASE_CLIENT_REUSE` (W0-2, LS-REQUEST-PATH-BLOCKING-02/-04 + CR-PERFORMANCE-03, read PER CALL) + knob `SUPABASE_POSTGREST_TIMEOUT_SECONDS` (default 8.0). Every authed request built a fresh `create_client` — ~215 ms of blocking TLS setup, ~450 ms for a user-scoped client — ON the event loop (`auth_service.py:256` constructs, `:257` is the `await run_db`), and no construction site passed `ClientOptions`, so every postgrest session inherited the library's **120 s read AND connect** default. **Effect ON:** `database_service` owns ONE lazily built `httpx.Client(timeout=Timeout(v, connect=3.0), http2=True, follow_redirects=True, limits=100/20)` threaded through a FRESH `ClientOptions(httpx_client=…)` per construction (a SHARED options object shares `.storage` — `supabase/_sync/client.py:72` shallow-copies — which is a cross-user JWT bleed). Only `httpx_client=` is passed: `postgrest_client_timeout`/`storage_client_timeout` are inert once a client is injected and emit a DeprecationWarning on the pinned 2.31.0. **The ANON client is NEVER memoised** — nine of its ten call sites are auth-STATE operations and supabase-py stamps the signed-in JWT onto the client's own `options.headers` on SIGNED_IN, while `sign_out()` reads the client's stored session and calls `admin.sign_out(token, scope="global")`, so a shared anon client would let one user's logout globally revoke another; a fresh client on the shared transport costs ~0.12 ms, so the memo bought nothing. Only the two SERVICE-ROLE clients are memoised. **Knob caveat:** the timeout is read ONCE, when the transport is built, so changing it needs a restart/redeploy — the FLAG itself is a true live kill-switch (the memoised admin client records the flag state it was built under and rebuilds on a flip). **Canary-watch:** the gotrue leg moves off httpx's 5.0 s default onto the knob (8 s) — a LOOSENING of the auth ceiling; and `/home/savings`' legacy unbounded scan inherits the 8 s bound with no per-route override (`#116` + migration `036`, still UNAPPLIED, is the real fix). The transport build fails open: one WARNING, then today's bare `create_client`.
- `ENABLE_UPSTASH_BOUNDED_TRANSPORT` (W0-3, LS-REQUEST-PATH-BLOCKING-03 = LS-CACHE-REDIS-01 = LS-CONCURRENCY-LIMITS-04) + knobs `UPSTASH_TIMEOUT_SECONDS` (2.0) / `UPSTASH_CONNECT_TIMEOUT_SECONDS` (1.0). The pinned `upstash-redis==1.7.0` builds `httpx.Client(timeout=None)` and defaults to `rest_retries=1` with a blocking `time.sleep(3)` between attempts, so a stalled Upstash REST endpoint parked a request-path thread for **13 s** (5 s + 3 s + 5 s) with no ceiling. The SDK exposes no timeout kwarg, so under the flag the client is built with `rest_retries=0` and its transport replaced after construction; the `redis://` fallback gets `socket_timeout`/`socket_connect_timeout`. Auth headers are passed per request by the SDK, so the swap keeps authentication. **⚠️ READ ONCE PER PROCESS:** the client is constructed at MODULE IMPORT, so this flag AND both knobs need a **restart/redeploy** — unlike every other flag in this file, a Railway flip does not take effect on the next request. Fails open on SDK drift: one WARNING, then today's plain client (never "no client", which would take caching offline process-wide). **Canary-watch:** `rest_retries=0` means a transient blip is no longer retried at all; and a slow-but-ALIVE Upstash (RTT > 2 s) now surfaces as a Redis error after 2 s instead of ~13 s, so the three consumers that already fail OPEN on that error hit it sooner — `auth_service._is_token_revoked` (`:434-441`, a revoked JWT is honoured for the rest of its 1 h blacklist TTL), the `api_budget_service` spend gates (`:247-249`, `:390-392`) and the circuit breaker (`:561-563`). Watch token revocation and paid-spend gates, not latency alone; `UPSTASH_TIMEOUT_SECONDS` is the lever if Upstash p99 sits near the bound.
- `ENABLE_PRICE_PARSE_OFFLOAD` (W0-4, LS-CONCURRENCY-LIMITS-01 + CR-PERFORMANCE-01, read PER CALL) — MEASURED: one `extract_price_from_html` over a foreign-currency JSON-LD Offer built **four** `BeautifulSoup` trees over the same bytes (its own at `:13056` plus three re-parses at `:10642`, one per `extract_jsonld_price` pass) under the shipped default `ENABLE_JSONLD_FIRST=ON`, and that pure-sync CPU-bound parse ran INLINE on the event loop at all three async sites (`price_service.fetch_page_price`, `structured_comparison_service._firecrawl_scraper` and `_scrapedo_scraper`): the loop was frozen for the whole parse (measured by R-W04: 300–602 ms per inline parse at the six sites below; a 50 ms heartbeat ticked **0** times). **Effect ON (as amended by R-W04, #196):** the soup is built once and threaded into all three JSON-LD passes; EVERY pure-sync parse site — the three original ones plus `fetch_iherb_price`, `fetch_bolo_price`, `fetch_boutiqaat_price`, `_try_pharmacy_urls`, `url_extraction_service.extract_with_ai` and `StructuredComparisonService._fetch_page_price` (which parsed INLINE on the loop; the earlier sentence that iHerb "already parses inside a run_in_executor lambda" was FALSE) — runs as ONE job on a dedicated, bounded `price-parse` `ThreadPoolExecutor` (`PRICE_PARSE_MAX_WORKERS`, default 4, clamped 1..16, unparsable/non-finite → 4, read ONCE when the pool is built) behind a per-event-loop `asyncio.Semaphore` of the same size acquired BEFORE submit (queued parses wait in asyncio, not in the executor), entered through `contextvars.copy_context().run` so the resolved-category ContextVar reaches the worker; `curl_fetch_html` and the firecrawl/scrapedo render legs hand at most `PRICE_FETCH_MAX_BYTES` = 3,000,000 chars to the parse (mirroring `curl_fetch_html_same_site`; the provider-attempt `html_kb` telemetry still reports the raw body). A source-level AST guard keeps the inline-parse allowlist EMPTY. Flag OFF: four soups, inline call, the whole body, byte-identical (corpus OFF OVERALL `9504e5a9…`, results `a1b3460c…` equal to a same-env `origin/main` run; ON `--compare` OFF: 0 of 1656 records differ; 11 corpus records exceed 3,000,000 chars and none moves under the cap). **Residual (measured):** offloaded parses of the largest (3.9–4.1 MB) pages still cost the loop 35–143 ms single and 171–534 ms with six at once (six median pages 142–174 ms) against 1,089 ms inline — the pool bounds it, it does not remove it. **Stated limit:** under the flag the 3 MB cap also applies to `_lazy_bh_pdp_backfill`'s regex scan of a curled search page (an href past 3,000,000 chars is no longer found — W0-4e). Harness: `scripts/verify_flag_byte_identity.py --flags-on <flags>` (restores the prior env, incl. unset) and `--compare <other-results.json>` (results-array sha equality + the first differing records; exit 1 on mismatch).

**W0 ACTIVATION ORDER (one canary per step, never batch):** (1) `ENABLE_OFFLOOP_DNS_RESOLVE` alone — it is the P0 and the only one that changes an unauthenticated front door; watch the `/url/*` 400 rate and Railway drain time. (2) `ENABLE_PRICE_PARSE_OFFLOAD` — pure latency/CPU placement, no result change (the corpus gate proves it, ON vs OFF 0/1656 with the 3 MB cap in place); size `PRICE_PARSE_MAX_WORKERS` (default 4) for the box and watch the price-parse pool's queue depth together with `/health` `loop_lag_ms` / `loop_lag_max_60s_ms` — the measured residual is 35–143 ms per large page even when offloaded (R-W04). (3) `ENABLE_SUPABASE_CLIENT_REUSE` — watch `/home/savings` and auth error rates for the two accepted ceiling changes; it is a live kill-switch if either bites. (4) `ENABLE_UPSTASH_BOUNDED_TRANSPORT` LAST and with a **restart**, watching token revocation and the paid-spend gates. Only after all four are ACTIVE and stable do the M13-W3 offload flags (`ENABLE_SYNC_DB_OFFLOAD`, `ENABLE_ASYNC_REDIS_OFFLOAD`) and then `#114` become safe to canary — before W0 they would move blocking work onto a loop that DNS alone could freeze for 11 s.

Railway MCP server is configured at project root (`.mcp.json`, stdio via `railway mcp`); no agent brief calls any of its tools (principle 10), and variables are read with the CLI only, by the orchestrator or Ahmed, with the names-only recipe in the `#98` paragraph above (a non-secret flag may be confirmed by its exact-line count; a value is never printed). The CLI and the MCP need a first-time `railway login` in a real terminal (interactive auth; cached in `%USERPROFILE%\.railway`).

**Price / cache-truth cluster flags (issues `#51`–`#57`, branch `feature/m22-price-truth-cluster` from `76ace90`, PR #136, 2026-09-05/07).** Seven default-OFF flags, each read PER CALL via `os.getenv` in ONE helper (`price_service.exact_gate_enabled` idiom; `product_data_service` delegates to the `price_service` definition lazily and fail-closed so no second env parse can drift). **Flag-OFF byte-identity gate PROVEN** on `2f67b70 (later commits touch `text_routes.py`/tests only; `price_service.py` byte-unchanged)`: `scripts/verify_flag_byte_identity.py` over `_proof` (414 pages / 1,656 calls) prints the SAME `OVERALL SHA256 ec120b8b3f2a2f65bfc1f068b877f183aad1ed3adf6619ebf8a9c555d69e2f81` at base and HEAD with all seven forced OFF. **Scope caveat (recorded, not hidden):** that harness exercises only `extract_price_from_html`, which never calls `fetch_iherb_price` nor the cache/L2/orchestrator paths this wave changes — the SHA proves no collateral damage to the extraction spine; each flag's byte-identity rests on its explicit flag-OFF pins in `tests/test_{iherb_page_currency_signal,converted_provenance_fanout,negcache_genuine_invalidation,genuine_price_clobber_guard,l2_promotion_remaining_ttl,regional_prices_category,flush_live_price_key}.py`. Comm gate (module-reference set, base `76ace90` vs HEAD): branch-only-NEW = ∅ (295-file module-reference set; the two later commits re-verified over the 187-file text_routes/price_service set: 9,621 passed / 1 baselined failure).
- `ENABLE_IHERB_PAGE_CURRENCY` (default OFF, `#52`) — `fetch_iherb_price` branches on a REAL page currency signal instead of the `_origin == currency` self-comparison that stamped EVERY iHerb price genuine `local_bhd`. **Effect ON:** rung 1 = the chosen card's own `meta[itemprop=priceCurrency]` (foreign → `_convert_to_bhd` + `converted_usd`; unreadable → pend); rung 2 = document-level microdata/og/JSON-LD evidence, which may only CONFIRM the storefront currency or PEND — it NEVER converts a card amount (a multi-product page's document tag is not scoped to the chosen card; fix-wave-2 `bf945cd`); a page that CONTRADICTS itself (≥2 distinct codes) PENDS instead of falling into the storefront-assumption arm (fix-wave-2); a silent page keeps the storefront currency exactly as today. Convertibility goes through `exchange_rate_service.is_convertible()` (the M21-W4 shared gate). **Flag OFF:** the assumption arm, byte-identical (explicit flag-OFF pins over every fixture). **Canary ALONE** — a currency-LABEL change on a genuine-KPI path. **Open product call (Ahmed):** `source_router.py` classes `iherb.com` tier `global`, and BOTH global-tier guards (`_is_genuine_bh_candidate`, the `_curl_scraper` downgrade) refuse a genuine label for such a host, yet the supplements tier stamps `bh.iherb.com` `local_bhd` (2026-06-14 ruling, `tests/test_iherb_native_bhd_label.py`) and bypasses both guards — one side is shipping a wrong provenance verdict; the per-card rung is also inert on both real-markup fixtures (a live capture must settle where iHerb puts `priceCurrency`).
- `ENABLE_CONVERTED_PROVENANCE_STAMP` (default OFF, `#51a`; parts b/c shipped earlier under `ENABLE_JSONLD_FIRST`) — the fan-out keeps `converted_usd` provenance on `_curl_scraper` / firecrawl / scrapedo candidates and in `_finalize_fan_winner`, so a CONVERTED price can no longer bank as a genuine-set method (7d TTL, genuine authority tier, genuine-BH-share KPI). **Effect ON:** a converted render candidate loses to a genuine BH page-scrape in `_select_best`, no longer satisfies `_confirmed` (so it cannot cancel pending genuine scrapers), caches 24h. **Why dark:** it changes which candidate wins the race and the KPI.
- `ENABLE_NEGCACHE_GENUINE_INVALIDATION` (default OFF, `#53`) — every genuine L1 persist (all SIX write sites through the shared `_cache_price_and_clear_sentinel`) deletes the 30d `nogenuine:{key}` sentinel via `_cache_delete_async` (honours `ENABLE_ASYNC_REDIS_OFFLOAD`). **Effect ON:** a genuine price found later un-suppresses the Tier-1.5 cascade for that key (spends Serper/Firecrawl budget on later requests). **Flag OFF:** exactly the one `_cache_set_async` each site made before; `should_cache_price` stays outside the writer, so an identity rejection still clears nothing.
- `ENABLE_GENUINE_PRICE_CLOBBER_GUARD` (default OFF, `#54`, ONE flag for both halves — they are one defect) — **L1:** the Tier-3 estimate terminal reads the existing entry and withholds BOTH the 12h L1 write and the L2 append when a GENUINE price already sits there (identity-revalidated with the read path's `_cache_price_identity_ok`, fix-wave-2 `0761ec8`; deliberately still holds under `nocache` — an estimate must never clobber a genuine price; the poisoned-entry remedy is the `#55` flush, not `nocache`), and the caller then ALSO withholds the 30d `nogenuine:` sentinel (`#54 × #53` composition fix, fix-wave-2 `9577626` — before it, the withheld estimate was still planted as a sentinel that outlived the 7d genuine entry by 23 days). **L2:** `get_cached_price` scans `_L2_PRICE_ROW_SCAN` = 5 rows and prefers the freshest GENUINE row over a newer estimate row (append-only table). **Why dark:** drops a write that used to happen and can return an older, more authoritative row. **ACTIVATION ORDER (three couplings):** (1) `ENABLE_L2_PROMOTION_REMAINING_TTL` with/before this flag — with the guard ON and the TTL flag OFF a 6d23h genuine L2 row is re-promoted into L1 for a FULL 7d (~14d serving age; measured); (2) `ENABLE_NEGCACHE_GENUINE_INVALIDATION` with/before this flag (it clears sentinels already standing from before either flag existed; the two now compose instead of undoing each other); (3) `ENABLE_FLUSH_LIVE_PRICE_KEY` ON (or the flush verified) BEFORE this flag reaches an environment where ops used `?nocache=true` to clear a bad price — `nocache` no longer replaces a genuine L1 entry with an estimate. **Coupling, not a fork:** the identity revalidation inherits `ENABLE_EXACT_PRICE_GATE` (ON in prod); with the exact gate OFF the revalidation is a no-op and the guard blocks on any genuine-method entry (pinned). **Known pin contradiction (P3, recorded):** with this flag set, `tests/test_price_cache_bust_probe.py::TestPriceReadBypass::test_bust_skips_price_redis_read` goes red because the guard reads the price key under `PRICE_CACHE_BUST` — CI runs flag OFF; the pin needs a flag-aware rewrite before activation. **Canary log lines:** `[PRICE] nogenuine sentinel NOT planted` (guard fired) and `[PRICE] tier-3 clobber guard STOOD DOWN for <key>` (a poisoned genuine-method entry the read path already discards — a steady stream means real cache poisoning).
- `ENABLE_L2_PROMOTION_REMAINING_TTL` (default OFF, `#57`) — L2→L1 promotion writes `max(0, price_cache_ttl(row) − row_age)` computed from the row `_select_price_row` actually SELECTED; a row past its window is served for this request but NOT promoted (never a zero/negative TTL). **Flag OFF:** the full TTL, no `fetched_at` parse, no extra key on the returned dict, `limit(1)`.
- `ENABLE_REGIONAL_PRICES_CATEGORY` (default OFF, `#56`) — `get_regional_prices` (`/text/prices`) infers the category BEFORE the six-way `_get_price` fan-out and threads it BY KEYWORD (the 6th positional is `nocache`), so category-gated adapters (nasser/bolo/boutiqaat) engage on this path and the cache key matches the compare path (electronics bare-`5G` drop in `size_variant_token`). **Flag OFF:** no `category` kwarg reaches `_get_price` at all; the only delta is the pure inference running before the gather.
- `ENABLE_FLUSH_LIVE_PRICE_KEY` (default OFF, `#55`) — `DELETE /api/v1/text/cache` deletes the ordered, de-duplicated set of L1 price keys the live path can write (the live size-aware key from the parser's `search_query` + canonicalized category, plus an optional `category` query param — read off `request.query_params` INSIDE the flag branch (2f67b70; a declared `Query` param changed the flag-OFF OpenAPI surface and 422'd over-length values, which the byte-identity corpus gate cannot see) — for the chip the comparison ran under, since the live path keys under the pair-resolved `category_used` = the chip whenever the LLM emitted "other" (410fc46); operator rule: `DELETE /api/v1/text/cache?q=<query>&category=<chip>`; the raw-`q` key; the legacy key), each key's `nogenuine:` sentinel, and the L2 `product_prices` rows (service-role client; `migrations/012` has no delete policy for the anon role). The response reports `existed`/`deleted` per key, `rows_deleted` (`None` = unknown, never 0), and `success` is True ONLY when Redis is configured AND every L1 delete AND every L2 delete succeeded (fix-wave-2). **UNFLAGGED repair in the same commit (M13-10 class):** the route 500'd on EVERY real call — `parse_product_query` returns `(result, usage)` and the route called `.get` on the tuple. **Flag OFF:** the three legacy keys, the legacy body shape, no existence probe, no Supabase call — byte-identical (an unknown `category` query param is ignored).

**M23 mobile wave flag (issue-free B2 from the 2026-09-02 mobile checkup, branch `feature/m23-mobile-w1`, PR #138, 2026-09-05/07).** The wave's 22 client-side tasks ship UNFLAGGED (RN has no per-call env flag, precedent M13-W4); the ONE backend change is dark:
- `ENABLE_STRICT_OPTIONAL_AUTH` (default OFF, B2, `app/api/auth_routes.py::get_optional_user`, read PER CALL via `os.getenv` in `strict_optional_auth_enabled()`) — **Effect ON:** an EXPIRED or invalid-signature Bearer on an auth-optional route raises 401 with the standard envelope (so the client interceptor refreshes once and retries with the new token, metered/persisted once) instead of silently downgrading the caller to ANONYMOUS (history row lost, personalization lost, usage unmetered — the checkup's B2 P1); an ABSENT header stays anonymous. Zero route files edited: every compare endpoint already takes `Depends(get_optional_user)`. **Flag OFF:** byte-identical — the only structural addition is `except HTTPException: raise`, unreachable OFF because `verify_token` swallows every exception to None (pinned: 10 flag-OFF pins green / 19 flag-ON pins red without the fix). **HARD PRECONDITIONS before activation:** (1) Session 65's `POST /auth/refresh` 503-for-upstream-failure fix (W1) must be live — today `/refresh` 401s on its OWN Supabase blip, so with this flag ON an auth outage becomes 401 → refresh 401 → `clearSession` → forced logout of every active user; (2) the camera `/image/identify` client path is a raw `fetch` with no 401→refresh→retry, so the first camera compare after token expiry would land on the generic empty state — add the retry (or route it through axios) first; (3) the M23 OTA must be on phones (the retry + boot-refresh semantics live in the client). **Canary log:** count 401s on auth-optional routes vs refresh successes; a rising ratio without a matching refresh success means the client retry is not landing. **UPDATE (SESSION 65 wave W1, unit W1-4, 2026-09-08): precondition (1) IS NOW LIVE** — `POST /auth/refresh` now maps the TRANSIENT upstream class (and only that class, marked `code: "UPSTREAM_UNAVAILABLE"` by `auth_service._categorize_auth_error`) to **503** with the structured detail `{"code": "REFRESH_UPSTREAM_UNAVAILABLE", "error": ...}`, while every other failure still 401s — and it ships UNFLAGGED precisely so this precondition is satisfied by the deploy, not by a flag flip.

**SESSION 65 wave W1 flag (unit W1-4 — logout that actually revokes; findings `LS-CACHE-REDIS-03` / `MB-NETWORK-CONTRACT-02`, branch `feature/s65-w1-4-auth-revocation`, 2026-09-08). The unit's REFRESH half is UNFLAGGED (see the `ENABLE_STRICT_OPTIONAL_AUTH` row above); only the LOGOUT half is dark:**
- `ENABLE_LOGOUT_UPSTREAM_REVOCATION` (default OFF, `app/services/auth_service.py::logout_user`, read PER CALL via `os.getenv` in `logout_upstream_revocation_enabled()` — the `price_service.exact_gate_enabled` idiom, so Railway flips it without a restart) — today `logout_user` blacklists the ACCESS token in Redis for 1 h and then calls `client.auth.sign_out()` on a FRESHLY BUILT anon client that holds NO session; gotrue reads the session off the client's own storage, so that call revokes nothing and the REFRESH token stays valid upstream — past the 1 h blacklist TTL a stolen refresh token still mints new sessions. **Effect ON:** when (and only when) the caller supplied a refresh token, logout does `client.auth.set_session(access_token, refresh_token)` and then `client.auth.sign_out({"scope": "local"})`, so the session just ended is actually revoked. **INERT until the client sends it:** the refresh token in the logout body is a CLIENT change that reaches devices only with the next `eas update`, so flag ON is a no-op for every build now on phones — which is the whole reason the flag exists. **CORRECTED by R-AUTH (#194, 2026-09-24): NO LONGER INERT.** With the flag ON the backend revokes by the ACCESS token — `admin.sign_out(access_token, "local")` through `run_db` regardless of the refresh token — and an EXPIRED bearer with a `refresh_token` body refreshes once (`set_session`, rotates) then signs out local and blacklists, budgeted at 10/minute per limiter key (its own bucket, checked after the expiry and token checks). With `ENABLE_PROXY_AWARE_RATELIMIT` ON that key is the caller-chosen leftmost `X-Forwarded-For` (the exposure `/auth/refresh` already has) — follow-up W1-9e BEFORE flipping this flag on a proxy-aware deployment. Unflagged alongside it: refresh failures are classified by exception TYPE (W1-4b), server-side Supabase clients never auto-refresh (`auto_refresh_token=False, persist_session=False`, W1-4c), and lockout arming is atomic with a TTL re-arm (W1-9b). **`scope="local"`, never `"global"` (binding ruling):** the finding is that the refresh token of the session just ended stays valid, and `local` closes exactly that; `global` would ALSO sign the user out on every OTHER device (tap logout on your phone, your tablet dies on its next refresh), which nobody asked for — "sign out everywhere" is a separate feature needing its own UI affordance and is Ahmed's call. Pinned both ways in `tests/test_auth_refresh_and_revocation.py` (assert the scope that reaches the SDK, AND assert a second device's refresh token still works after the first logs out). **The options DICT is mandatory:** on the pinned `supabase`/`supabase-auth` 2.31.0 the signature is `sign_out(options: Optional[SignOutOptions] = None)`, so `sign_out(scope="local")` raises `TypeError` — which `logout_user`'s broad `except` would swallow while still reporting success, i.e. a silent no-op that LOOKS fixed — and the SDK's own body is `options or {"scope": "global"}`, so passing NOTHING signs the user out on every device. **`set_session` side effects, accepted and documented:** it decodes the access token (`access_token.split(".")[1]` → `decode_jwt`) so a non-JWT string RAISES, and on an ALREADY-EXPIRED access token (common — clients log out with whatever they hold) it calls `_refresh_access_token`, which ROTATES the refresh token; otherwise it makes a `get_user` round trip. So logout now costs ONE upstream call it never made before, and on the expired path revocation happens via that rotation plus the `sign_out` on the NEW session rather than `sign_out` on the old one — the old refresh token is dead either way, which is what the finding requires. **Failure handling:** the catch stays broad and the route still returns `{"success": True}` (the local blacklist has been written and the client ignores this response), but a failed upstream leg is logged at WARNING naming the leg and the exception type and explicitly stating the session was NOT revoked upstream — never let a `TypeError`-shaped mistake pass as success again. **Flag OFF, or no refresh token in the body:** today's exact path (bare `sign_out()`, no `set_session`, the same 1 h Redis blacklist write, the same response body), test-pinned. **Client half shipped (PR #140, OTA-gated):** `authService.logout()` sends the stored `qaren_refresh_token` as `{ refresh_token }` (`{}` when nothing is stored, so the no-body path is unchanged) — the upstream `sign_out({"scope": "local"})` fires only for builds carrying that client half, so the next `eas update` is the real activation lever, not the env var.


**SESSION 65d wave flags (W3-2, W1-5, W4-5 merged 2026-09-11 as #155 / #156 / #158; W1-6 #157 is ops-only, no flag).** All three default OFF. `ENABLE_COMPARISON_ID_ECHO` and `ENABLE_SHOWABLE_NAME_IDENTITY` are read PER CALL (the `price_service.exact_gate_enabled` idiom, so a Railway flip needs no restart); **`ENABLE_LIMITER_ENDPOINT_KEY` is READ ONCE at `Limiter` construction and needs a RESTART/REDEPLOY — flipping the variable alone changes nothing** (pinned by test; same restart class as W0-3's Upstash transport).
- `ENABLE_COMPARISON_ID_ECHO` (W3-2, `MB-NETWORK-CONTRACT-07`, `app/api/text_routes.py::comparison_id_echo_enabled`) + knob `COMPARISON_ID_PERSIST_TIMEOUT_SECONDS` (default 5.0; garbage, non-positive AND non-finite values fall back to 5.0 — `float()` accepts `inf`/`nan`, and `inf` would silently reinstate the unbounded wait the knob exists to close). **ON:** POST/GET `/text/compare` AWAIT the `comparisons` insert, bounded by `asyncio.wait_for`, and echo `comparison_id` (a timeout or failed insert echoes `comparison_id: null` — the key is always present); the SSE stream runs the same bounded insert immediately before its terminal pair under the M18 CD-interactions-01 gate terms (`user_id and not client_gone and not had_error`) and BOTH `settle_complete`/`complete` carry the key. A non-null id always names a row that exists, so no dangling id is possible. **OFF (prod today):** the byte-identical pre-W3-2 path on all three routes — `fire_and_forget(save_comparison_and_track_cohort(...))` under today's labels, no key. **ACTIVATION PRECONDITION: flip ONLY AFTER `ENABLE_SUPABASE_CLIENT_REUSE` is ON** (with client reuse OFF the insert's only ceiling is supabase-py's 120 s default), and preferably after `ENABLE_SYNC_DB_OFFLOAD` (the 5 s bound is INERT while `run_db` blocks the loop). Cost measured: the await adds one insert RTT (285 ms p50) and <1 ms of its own, on a compare whose p50 is 22 s. It WIDENS the latch→terminal-yield window from microseconds to that RTT, so a client dropping inside it is persisted AND metered with no refund (reproduced; flag-gated). Mobile bounds for context: `COMPARE_TIMEOUT_MS` 35 s REST, `STREAM_WATCHDOG_MS` 60 s (`api.ts:43/:47`).
- `ENABLE_LIMITER_ENDPOINT_KEY` (W1-5, `app/middleware/rate_limiter.py::_endpoint_key_style_enabled`). **ON:** slowapi `key_style="endpoint"`, so the SEVEN path-parameterised routes bucket per caller per view function instead of per resolved URL — `GET`/`DELETE /api/v1/comparisons/{comparison_id}`, `GET /api/v1/referrals/invite/{share_token}`, `POST …/{share_token}/quiz`, `POST /api/v1/share/{comparison_id}`, `GET /api/v1/share/{token}`, and `GET /api/v1/text/prices/{product}` while `ENABLE_PAID_ROUTE_METERING` is OFF. That closes the vary-the-parameter escape (35 distinct share tokens = 35 × a 30/minute limit today; ON, the 31st request is a 429). **OFF:** the `Limiter` is constructed with exactly today's three kwargs and no `key_style`. With `ENABLE_PAID_ROUTE_METERING` ON the prices route's live decorator is a `shared_limit` whose explicit `scope` ignores `key_style`, so the flag is a no-op there. **Sequence AFTER `ENABLE_PROXY_AWARE_RATELIMIT`** if `/share/*` matters: while the proxy-aware key is OFF the key is still the shared Railway edge-proxy IP, so ON collapses each affected route to ONE deployment-wide bucket.
- `ENABLE_SHOWABLE_NAME_IDENTITY` (W4-5, `PO-PRICE-TRUTH-04`, `app/services/price_service.py::showable_name_identity_enabled`). **ON:** the sample/decant guard inside `is_price_showable` reads `title or name` instead of `title` alone. Every page-scrape producer (JSON-LD, OG, microdata, WooCommerce, RSC — all six stamp sites, gated on `ENABLE_EXACT_PRICE_GATE`) stamps the listing identity under `name` and never `title`, so a page-scraped `"'Ilm Sample & Decants by Xerjoff"` at 94 BHD was invisible to the guard and SHOWED as the product's price. `title` stays authoritative (a present `title` short-circuits the fallback), so adapter-rung verdicts are unchanged in BOTH states. **Flip immediately after merge — no ordering coupling.** With `ENABLE_EXACT_PRICE_GATE` OFF nothing is stamped and the fallback has nothing to read. NOT covered, each its own row: `PO-PRICE-TRUTH-04b` (the Firecrawl/Scrape.do/fan-out L2 content-safety surface reads `title` only), `PO-PRICE-TRUTH-04c` (the low-fragrance floor still receives `title` only and OVER-rejects honest small listings under `name`), and the guard's silence (no `guard_rejected` stamp on a sample pend, contrary to its own docstring).

**Shutdown drain (W1-6, `LS-FAILURE-MODES-COST-05`, merged as #157 — NO flag: ops behaviour that only ever waits LONGER).** `fire_and_forget` now registers its task in `_BACKGROUND_TASKS` (the done-callback discards FIRST and unconditionally, so a cancelled task cannot leak) and a lifespan shutdown handler registered AFTER W1-10's `_stop_loop_lag_heartbeat` awaits the in-flight set with `asyncio.wait(pending, timeout=DRAIN_TIMEOUT)` — never `gather`, which would end the drain on the first exception. `DRAIN_TIMEOUT = 8.0`, read at call time; stragglers are logged at WARNING BY LABEL and ABANDONED, never cancelled (cancelling would turn a slow refund that would still have landed into a guaranteed loss). This covers all SEVEN `refund_comparison_credit` fire-and-forget sites by construction, and is a hard precondition for the `ENABLE_ASYNC_REDIS_OFFLOAD` canary.
