measured: the global fragrance discovery query loses `site:fragrantica.com` in both catalog states, `score_source(<fragrantica url>)` 1.0 -> 0.5, `registry_tier` `global` -> None), while `SOURCE_REGISTRY` and `_LITERAL_ROWS` stay byte-identical (the fragrantica.com `Source` literal in `_LITERAL_ROWS` is never deleted here; that is the later R-A1 PR); the filtered list is memoised per process with a STRONG reference to the registry object it filtered (ruling B4: a hit needs the same object, so a replaced registry is always re-filtered); (2) `price_service.validate_scrape_url` returns False for a denied URL: the ONE never-fetched chokepoint every escalation, BH-tier, official-tier and organic-harvest candidate passes (ruling B2), so the "as if absent" `registry_tier` None can never turn a fail-closed global-tier refusal into an off-registry fetch (a `/bh-en/` Fragrantica link is never harvested, pinned in both states); (3) `rating_service` never selects a denied rating source by name or link: `get_rating_tier` returns 3 before the `RATING_TIER_2` substring and the `.com` / `.ae` rule (OQ-8), `extract_rating_from_shopping` skips it before any tier bucket and before the Google consensus rule, `collect_retailer_ratings` drops it; `RATING_TIER_1/2/3` and `LUXURY_FASHION_RETAILERS` stay byte-identical; (4) `review_service` (ruling B1, post-LLM, no prompt digest moves): `build_retailer_quotes_from_reviews` never quotes a cited result on a denied host and `clean_review_citations` writes no `Per <denied domain>:` attribution (the marker is still stripped); both decide on the cited link's parsed hostname through `is_denied_url`, so a userinfo or backslash link is denied too (fix-round ruling F1-4); (5) the descriptor probe (`search_descriptor_service._deny`) refuses every denied host before a fetch; (6) `image_service._is_valid_image_url` treats an image on a denied host as invalid in every tier, and the Tier 1.5 piggyback override in `structured_comparison_service._fetch_product_data` goes through that validator, reached as the module attribute `image_service._is_valid_image_url` so a patch of it takes effect (ruling C4, fix-round ruling F1-6; it strips before the scheme test, so ON a whitespace-padded page image is also taken where OFF it is not, and it is stored stripped); (7) `price_service.is_counterfeit_listing` matches a keyword as a whole token plus an optional plural `s` / `es` with LETTERS-only boundaries (ruling C1: "infused", "focused", "unused", "copyright" no longer drop a listing; "used tester", "replicas", "dupes", "pre-owned", "1:1replica", "Replica1" still do), keeps "copycat" counterfeit through `_CF_V2_EXTRA_TOKENS` (ruling C2), and ignores `replica` when the title carries `margiela` as a whole token (`COUNTERFEIT_BRAND_LINE_ALLOWLIST`; gold frag-018 "Maison Margiela Replica Jazz Club" now prices); (8) `price_service.is_luxury_brand` matches `lv` only as a whole token (`LV`, `L.V.`, `L.V`, `LV.`, `LV-Monogram`), never inside Silver, Velvet, Calvin, Elvive, Volvo, LVMH (gold supp-021, fash-015, hair-005/010/012); `COUNTERFEIT_KEYWORDS` (21) and `LUXURY_BRAND_KEYWORDS` (29) are unchanged; (9) the Serper-Shopping door (fix-round ruling F1-1: Shopping rows are priced from the payload and never fetched, so the chokepoint of item 2 does not cover them): `price_service.extract_price_from_shopping` and the scs re-selection stash `_seed_shortcircuit_candidates` drop a Shopping row whose link host or source name is denied (`_drop_denied_shopping_rows` over `_shopping_row_denied`, a new list, the shared `_shopping_items_cache` list untouched) BEFORE any currency, evidence or short-circuit logic, so such a row is never a candidate, never labelled `local_bhd` or `converted_usd` and never a stash seed, in both `ENABLE_SHOPPING_CURRENCY_TRUTH` states and for every ask (a pool of only denied rows yields no Shopping price); the W4-1 evidence helper `_shopping_bh_host_evidence` returns the new reason `denied_host` before every rung for a denied link; one INFO line per extraction call that dropped a row, `[DATA_GUARDS_V2] shopping rows dropped (denied host) count=<n> by=link|source host=<hostname>` (constants, an int and a hostname only; the stash is silent because the front door logged the same pool, and the W4-7 listing-count replay filters the line), and the W4-7 identity-matched listing count no longer counts such a row. The guards also move everything that reads them (ruling C8): `strict_title_match` (11 modules incl. `rating_service`), the response chokepoint `is_price_showable` through the designer low-price floor (a Calvin Klein fragrance no longer takes the 25 BHD / 100 ml floor), the pair-size fairness functions `effective_pair_size_ml` / `target_pair_size_ml` / `reselect_to_target_size` through `_is_designer_fragrance_name`, and the shopping candidate sort and flagship bonus. **Flag OFF:** byte-identical (pinned by `tests/test_s81_d03_deny_and_guards.py`, 292 nodes since the rebase round (288 at fix round 1 plus the four MIN-RV-3 caplog rows that pin the silent stash): P1-P14 and the F1 pins with the full configuration (`ENABLE_URL_MODE_V2=true`, `ENABLE_LONGEST_HOST_MATCH` both states, a synthetic registry carrying one denied row of every selector shape, every falsy flag spelling): `_routable_registry() is SOURCE_REGISTRY`, the denied rows flow through every consumer, the fragrantica rating is tier 2 and selected, the descriptor list is the two-host tuple, every guard verdict equals base, a fimgs.net image is valid, the piggyback keeps its inline test, the Fragrantica quote and attribution are shown (a userinfo link too, attributed by its netloc as at 5244f716), a Fragrantica `/bh-en/` Shopping row is priced `converted_usd` with the base reason `global_tier` under `ENABLE_SHOPPING_CURRENCY_TRUTH` ON and `local_bhd` under it OFF, the cheapest denied Shopping row wins its pool and the stash seeds every row; plus the W4-13 flag-OFF ledger, the FY25 nine digests and the 11 adapter files of ruling B5). **Stated limits:** (1) the URL half is unit D03b (ruling R-D03-1): until D03b lands a pasted link on a denied host is still fetched by URL mode (base and V2), a redirect hop onto one is followed and `normalize_image_url` can take a fimgs.net image; `url_routes.py` / `url_extraction_service.py` are untouched (pinned by P14); (2) denied-host text from Serper organic results still reaches the specs / reviews / Tier-3 image prompts and the Tier-2 GPT organic price prompt (`structured_comparison_service._get_price` calls `extract_price` over the `search_price_organic` results; the retailer / url that prompt returns carries no deny check) (unit D03c, ruling R-D03-2; only the Tier-3 OUTPUT is refused here; MIN-RV-1); (3) the `omega` (Omega-3) and `tiffany` (Tiffany biscuits) luxury substring false positives are untouched (unit D03d, R-D03-3); "REPLICA Jazz Club" without the brand word still drops, and so does any title carrying "homage", "inspired", "generic", "alternative" or "vintage" as a whole word (Amouage Homage Attar is a genuine product; OQ-5, spec L8); a fake that names Margiela and `replica` but no other keyword passes; (4) the registrable-label rule is a stdlib heuristic (GC-3): exotic multi-label public suffixes are not recognised (the 26-host tuple still catches the listed hosts by suffix); (5) `registry_tier(<denied host>)` is None ON, so the global-tier genuine-label refusals no longer see it, and no path turns that into a fetch or a label: the never-fetched chokepoint of item 2 keeps a denied URL unfetched, and the Shopping door of item 9 drops a denied Shopping row before the W4-1 evidence rule (fix-round ruling F1-1; at the GREEN head, before that fix, both round-1 adversaries measured a Fragrantica `/bh-en/` Shopping row relabelled `converted_usd` -> `local_bhd` under `ENABLE_SHOPPING_CURRENCY_TRUTH` ON, and the row was priced with the fragrantica.com url in every state); (6) direct `SOURCE_REGISTRY` readers outside `source_router` (the admin by_source probe list, the magento currency lookup, the curl timeout, the scs BH-locale memo `_bh_locale_known_retail_domains`) are untouched: the BH-locale memo still lists fragrantica.com, so a `/bh-en/` Fragrantica organic link reaches the off-registry rung and is refused only by the chokepoint of item 2 (review RC1); the other three are inert for the one denied row; the filtered-registry memo does not see an in-place same-object mutation of `SOURCE_REGISTRY` (nothing mutates it); (7) the V2 counterfeit regexes are compiled at import from the keyword literals (a runtime mutation of the set is not seen ON); (8) `scripts/verify_flag_byte_identity.py` is blind to this unit (its extraction path reaches none of the edited bodies); (9) never set the flag in a local `.env`: exactly three unedited pins encode the OFF rule and go red ON (measured at the GREEN head over the whole 409-file comm set with the flag exported for each runner call only, and again at the fix-round head over the two pin groups and the 38-file Shopping set): `tests/test_rating_tiers.py::TestExpandedTiers::test_fragrantica_is_tier_2`, `tests/test_retailer_quotes_from_reviews.py::TestRetailerQuotesGraceful::test_bare_numeric_citation_also_mapped`, `tests/test_fragrance_content_quality.py::test_review_praise_no_interior_per_domain_artifact` (the later R-A1 PR re-pins them; `tests/test_review_paraphrase.py` stays green ON: its inputs carry the attribution text literally); CI runs flag OFF; (10) a rollback is per call for NEW computations only: values computed ON stay in L1 for their TTL (price 24 h, genuine 7 d, Tier-3 estimate 12 h) and a 30-day `nogenuine:` sentinel planted under OFF still suppresses Tier 1.5 on previously compared keys after the flip (ruling C9); (11) ON a fragrance compare's global discovery window holds 3 domains instead of 4 (`site:amazon.com OR site:sephora.com OR site:walmart.com`; no replacement row exists in either catalog state; spec L10); the Serper call count is unchanged; (12) `LVs Neverfull` is luxury at base and not ON (the plural is not the whole token `lv`; review correction 18); (13) Salvatore Ferragamo is in neither brand table and was luxury / designer at base only through the `lv` substring inside "salvatore": ON it loses `is_luxury_brand`, the designer low-price floor (an 8 BHD decant-class listing of a Ferragamo 100 ml is no longer floored) and the flagship pair basis, and "Creed Silver Mountain Water" keeps the designer floor (creed is a fragrance house) but loses `is_luxury_brand`; a home for Ferragamo is follow-up D03d (MIN-4); (14) the backslash rule of `is_denied_url` is deliberately conservative: an authority carrying a backslash is denied when a banned label appears anywhere in it, even where a WHATWG parser would read another host; percent-encoded and IDNA-mapped host spellings (for example a percent-encoded first letter or a fullwidth letter) are not normalised and are not denied; a tab inside the host is (MIN-RV-2); the list is a policy list, not a security boundary (`url_validator` uses the same parser); (15) "one INFO line per request" is one line per `extract_price_from_shopping` call, so a compare logs at most one line per Shopping pool it prices (a request-scoped latch needs a request context these files do not own); pre-existing and not D03: `clean_review_citations` copies `reviews` shallowly and rewrites `review_summary` in place, so a second call on the same dict loses the citation markers (MIN-7, a follow-up); (16) denied Shopping rows still reach `cross_validate_specs_with_shopping` (spec `verified` marks) and the fact-check price median, which read `_shopping_items_cache` directly; no display surface is involved (MIN-RV-4, a follow-up). **Precondition:** none to merge; flip after approval, the owner's explicit yes, on `web` AND `price-warmer` in the SAME canary window (R-D03-4 / OQ-7). **Canary (never-seen pairs or `nocache=true`, C9):** the global discovery query in the Railway log carries no `site:fragrantica.com`; zero `rating_source` naming Fragrantica / Parfumo / Basenotes and zero `retailer_quotes` / `Per fragrantica.com:` lines in fresh fragrance compares; zero `fimgs.net` in `products[i].image_url`; zero Shopping `price.retailer` / `price.url` on a denied host and the count of `[DATA_GUARDS_V2] shopping rows dropped` lines; the counterfeit-rejection rate per category before vs after (expect a small drop concentrated in "infused" / Margiela titles); the shopping-price coverage and the per-pair size basis on Calvin Klein / Elvive / Centrum Silver / Salvatore Ferragamo / Creed Silver Mountain Water pairs. **Rollback:** unset the variable on both services (per call, immediate for new computations; see limit 10).

**SESSION 81 flag (S81-03 LLM-LEDGER-V2, 2026-10-23; default OFF, read PER CALL; a new name, so unset in production by construction (Railway not read); spec `.qa-s80/S81-03_UNIT_SPEC.md` of the unit worktree with review corrections C1-C14, rulings R-S03-1..R-S03-10 / BL1-BL5 (`specs/RULINGS_S81_03.md`), RED-gate rulings G-1..G-10 (`specs/GATE_S81_03.md`) and the fix-round-1 rulings F1-1..F1-10 / R-F1-A..R-F1-E (`specs/RULINGS_FIX1_S81_03.md`) in `docs/investigations/2026-10-22-session-81-state/`):**
- `ENABLE_LLM_LEDGER_V2` (S81-03, `app/services/llm_pricing.py::llm_ledger_v2_enabled`, a stdlib leaf over the untouched `openai_pricing.py`; every consumer reaches it by module attribute, `from app.services import llm_pricing as _llm_pricing`, ruling BL4). **Effect ON:** one function prices any provider (`price_call`: the OpenAI rows of `openai_pricing` by delegation, bit-identical to the OpenAI ledger; claude-haiku-5-5 0.10 / 0.50 USD per MTok, cache read 0.01, 5-minute write 0.125, 1-hour write 0.20, and above 100,000 prompt tokens (input + cache read + cache writes, strictly) the whole request at 0.50 / 2.50 with the DERIVED cache rates 0.05 / 0.625 / 1.00 (ruling R-S03-4); claude-sonnet-5-5 2 / 10, cache read 0.10, writes 2.50 / 4.00, flat up to 200,000 prompt tokens and unpriced above (gate G-2; each model's threshold has ONE source in the leaf: the Haiku entry of `LONG_CONTEXT_THRESHOLD_PROMPT_TOKENS` is derived from the tier row's `threshold_prompt_tokens` and the Sonnet entry lives only in that mapping, engineering m3); Batch halves token charges, never the 0.01-per-request web search fee; any other id or a non-int count = null, never 0; the platform.claude.com pricing page read 2026-10-22, research digest R1). `build_comparison_response` writes `metadata.llm` beside `metadata.openai` on all three build paths (REST, stream `complete`, hard-cap partial): the SUMMARY only (R-S03-1 / G-4: exactly `schema_version, calls, priced_calls, unpriced_calls, basis`, the seven token sums `prompt / completion / input / output / cache_read / cache_write_5m / cache_write_1h _tokens`, `web_search_requests, cost_usd, cost_complete, by_provider, by_model, price_table_as_of`; no per-call records, about 1 KB, bounded by the distinct models), equal to `metadata.openai` field for field for OpenAI traffic (built from the bound ledger when it summarises to the handed summary, else mapped from it), with the non-OpenAI records of a bound llm ledger merged in and any OpenAI-provider record there dropped (R-S03-7); the camera route merges its vision call into it under the `metadata.openai` condition. `_track_gpt_cost` (the unit's only `structured_comparison_service.py` hunk) prices each tracked call from the bound ledger entry it came from (the first unclaimed entry with equal prompt / completion tokens, each claimed once, entries never mutated; claims in a bounded module registry keyed by the identity of the bound ledger lists, 256 entries, gate G-1), and the builder ADDS the list price of every bound OpenAI-ledger entry no tracked call claimed (`llm_pricing.unclaimed_openai_cost_usd`, R-S03-2 / G-3) to `metadata.total_cost`, so `metadata.total_cost` and `search_logs.cost` (success rows) carry every call on the bound ledger at list price plus Serper and Tier 1.5 (the RED gate's measured fixture, cited as a measurement and not a pinned literal: 0.016634 -> 0.031992, the gpt-4o verdict having been priced at mini rates, 16.7x under; through the camera route with a real compare 0.016769 -> 0.032127, pinned by I6); the vision and variant-disambiguation costs use the vision / standard model's row (an unpriced model -> the base estimate); `ModelRouterService.get_model` returns a claude verdict id without reading the 4o counter and `record_usage(..., provider=None)` never increments it for a call served by another provider; `/api/v1/admin/costs` (`summary["llm"]`) and `/costs/api` (`llm`) add an all-provider block (a separate paged read `id,created_at,full_response->metadata->llm` on the endpoint's own client, the `_aggregate_openai` rules: null when nothing is recorded, never 0; `LLM_COST_NOTE`) that `costs.html` renders. The five wrapped ON bodies (builder, unclaimed, track, router x2, camera) fall back to the base value with ONE WARNING `[LLM-LEDGER] <site> failed type=<TypeName> -- base value` (R-S03-5); the three `openai_service` ON bodies (the V2 and V1 vision costs, the disambiguation cost) use getattr / isinstance only and cannot raise; a non-dict usage raises exactly as at base (C9). **Flag OFF:** byte-identical on every body and stored row: no `metadata.llm`, `metadata.total_cost` the base mini estimate, the base vision / disambiguation costs, the base router counter, the base admin queries and keys, and no `llm_pricing` function other than the flag reader is called (the TG site imports the module on every call; pinned by `tests/test_s81_s03_llm_ledger_v2.py` B4 / B4s / B5 / C3 / C6 / D3 / D5b / D6b / E4 / E5b / G3 / I2 / I6 OFF / U4 with the unit state primed and recorders on every leaf function, by the nine FY25 digests with the flag in `UNIT_ENV`, the W4-13 ledger and `tests/test_cost_meter_s74.py`); `openai_pricing.py` and `guarded_llm_create` are untouched (K2 / K1). **Unflagged (no-op on every row written OFF, R-A1(c)):** the public share view strips `metadata.llm` with `metadata.openai`; `record_usage` gains the additive keyword no caller passes; `costs.html` gains a hidden empty container. **Migration 050** `public.llm_spend_ledger` (one row per batch or cron job's calls to one provider and model, built by `llm_pricing.spend_ledger_row`; RLS on, no policy, append-only BY GRANT: `REVOKE ALL ... FROM PUBLIC, anon, authenticated, service_role` then `GRANT SELECT, INSERT ... TO service_role`, ruling R-S03-3; no user data) is written, NOT applied, and inert until S81-16 / S81-22 write to it; apply any time with `APPLY_050_1_PRECHECK` / `2_ONE_PASTE` / `3_POSTCHECK` (session-81 state folder). **Stated limits:** (1) the cost truth is half closed: failure rows keep the top-level service total, so they carry the tracked calls at ledger list price (the TG site prices them under ON) but not the unclaimed add (untracked calls and the error-path zero usages of the `extraction_service` except arms, C5); (2) OpenAI records carry no role, latency or stop reason (the capture point is `guarded_llm_create`); (3) the llm ledger is bound by S81-06 (`start_llm_ledger` beside each `start_openai_ledger`), so `metadata.llm` holds OpenAI calls only until then; (4) the match is by (prompt, completion) tokens: two calls with identical counts on different models can swap prices (the sum stays exact when both are tracked); (5) the Haiku long-context tier, the Sonnet 200,000 boundary and every Anthropic row are re-verified by S81-07's pricing read BEFORE the first Anthropic flip and a difference lands as a pure-defect data PR; both boundaries are pinned (A9: Haiku 100,000 flat, 100,001 tier; A9b: Sonnet 150,000 and 200,000 flat, 200,001 and 199,999 + 2 cache read unpriced); (6) no live price fetch: every rate is a literal with a read date; (7) an Anthropic usage without the `cache_creation` breakdown counts every cache write as 5-minute; (8) the Batch rate applies only to records flagged `batch` (none before S81-16); (9) `metadata.llm` adds about 1 KB per persisted comparison; (10) the admin `llm` block exists only while the flag is ON; (11) Link-mode compares are never persisted (#340) and no admin view reads `llm_spend_ledger` yet; (12) K1 is an `ast.dump` hash on CPython 3.12 that S81-04 re-records; (13) `inference_geo` us (1.1x) is not modelled; (14) under ON a claude verdict id skips the OpenAI 4o cap with no daily bound until S81-04 lands; (15) the claims registry keeps its 256-ledger bound: more than 256 compares in flight on one worker would evict a live claims entry and could double-count that request's tracked calls (follow-up: size the bound from `UVICORN_LIMIT_CONCURRENCY`); (16) never set the flag in a local `.env` or shell: `tests/test_cost_meter_s74.py::TestCostMeterS74::test_cm10_admin_costs_truncated_flag_and_range_contract` and `::test_cm10b_admin_costs_1001_row_page_counts_as_full` go red (the ON llm read adds a second range sequence); CI runs with it unset. **Preconditions:** none to merge; S81-04 SPEND-BREAKER is a PRECONDITION for `ENABLE_ANTHROPIC_VERDICT`, and S81-04 must not register a `PROVIDER_CONFIGS` entry named `llm` (the admin block reserves the name). **Follow-up contract for S81-06 (F1-9):** bind the llm ledger (`start_llm_ledger`) BEFORE any tracked call and in the parent task, never inside a gather child: the claims registry is keyed by the (openai, llm) ledger-list identity pair, so a late or child-task binding doubles every earlier tracked call in `metadata.total_cost`; the unclaimed add covers OpenAI-ledger entries only, so a Claude call reaches `metadata.total_cost` only when its caller tracks a usage dict with the normalised `prompt_tokens` / `completion_tokens`. **Canary (after approval, the owner's explicit yes, its own window):** three never-compared pairs; `metadata.llm.cost_usd` == `metadata.openai.cost_usd`, `metadata.total_cost` above its pre-flip level by the verdict and Tier-3 list-price delta, and the admin analytics series that sum `search_logs.cost` (`analytics_service.get_daily_stats`, `get_cost_trends`) stepping up about 1.9x at the flip (0.031992 / 0.016634 on the fixture): a truthful change, not a regression; `/admin/costs` `llm` block present, zero `[LLM-LEDGER]` WARNING lines in the Railway log. **Rollback:** unset the variable (per call, immediate); rows written during the window keep `metadata.llm` and the public share view still strips it.

**SESSION 81 flag (ST1 LOOP-STALL, 2026-10-23; default OFF, read PER CALL; a new name, so unset in production by construction (Railway not read); spec `.qa-s80/ST1_UNIT_SPEC.md` of the unit worktree as amended by its review, RULINGS_ST1 R-ST1-1..R-ST1-8, the RED gate GATE_ST1 GT-1..GT-10 and the fix-round rulings RULINGS_FIX1_ST1 F1-1..F1-10 (`docs/investigations/2026-10-22-session-81-state/specs/`)):**
- `ENABLE_USAGE_PATH_OFFLOOP` (ST1, reader `app/utils/redis_offloop.py::usage_path_offloop_enabled`, read PER CALL as the FIRST statement of `usage_service.get_usage_status` and `check_usage_allowed` and once per `review_service.get_reviews` call; dispatcher `run_redis_offloop_pool` in the same leaf: one job on the shared `redis-offloop` pool whatever `ENABLE_PRICE_PATH_REDIS_OFFLOOP` says; `run_redis_offloop` and `app/utils/db_offload.py` byte-unchanged). **Problem (measured at 95cf6f86):** `get_usage_status` (the `/usage/status` body, read on every Home and Paywall mount by build 3) ran its two Upstash counter GETs synchronously ON the event loop in every flag state (neither `ENABLE_ASYNC_REDIS_OFFLOAD` nor the S77 flag reaches them), and its Supabase reads (the users tier row plus the lazy referral-bonus reset UPDATE when due, the active `referral_redemptions` sum) run inline whenever `ENABLE_SYNC_DB_OFFLOAD` is OFF (production); `review_service.get_reviews` wrote its reviews L1 inline after every cold extraction. Production showed `usage_status` p50 4,164 ms in the 14-day Sentry trace search, and the single trace 5915a2b6 showed a `usage_status` of 4,266 ms whose child I/O summed to under 0.8 s (session-80 prod_evidence findings 8/9): the route is mostly a VICTIM of other loop blockers, and ST1 removes only its own loop occupancy. **Effect ON:** `get_usage_status` (called by `usage_routes.py`) and `check_usage_allowed` (the only one of the two with no production caller) compute the two date keys on the loop, then run ONE `asyncio.gather` of two `run_redis_offloop_pool(_get_redis_count, key)` jobs, listed FIRST so that with `ENABLE_SYNC_DB_OFFLOAD` OFF both GETs are in flight on the pool while the inline Supabase reads run (fix round 1, F1-8), and the existing async helpers `_get_user_tier_info` / `_get_active_referral_bonus` (byte-unchanged, through `run_db`), then build the response with the ONE pure compute body (`_usage_status_body` / `_usage_allowed_body(user_info, bonus, daily_used, monthly_used)`), which the flag-OFF path calls too (R-ST1-3); the fork's `try` wraps only the dispatch and the gather of the reads, and the body runs after it, so a compute-body exception (e.g. a NULL `lifetime_comparisons_used`) propagates exactly as at base with no WARNING and no second read pass (F1-3); a dispatch failure (a synchronous raise first closes the coroutines already built, GT-6) logs ONE WARNING `[USAGE-OFFLOOP] dispatch failed fn=<fn> type=<Type> -- inline fallback` and runs the base inline path (same response); a cancel propagates with no fallback. `get_reviews` reads the L1 on the pool, schedules the L2 save first, then makes each post-extraction L1 write (the baseline write and the consult / YouTube re-cache writes) a DURABLE, NON-AWAITED pool job on a `copy.deepcopy` snapshot taken on the loop, chained per request inside its `fire_and_forget` task (`reviews.l1_write`; each write waits for the previous write's task INSIDE that task, never on the request path, GT-2), so the response never waits on the pool and the last snapshot wins as at base; a snapshot or dispatch failure logs `[USAGE-OFFLOOP] reviews L1 write dispatch failed type=<Type>` and is swallowed; the L2-promotion write stays the inline base call (RO17). **Production shape (R-ST1-1):** with `ENABLE_SYNC_DB_OFFLOAD` OFF (production today) ST1 alone moves only the two Upstash counter GETs (and the reviews L1 calls) off the loop; the Supabase reads leave the loop only once `ENABLE_SYNC_DB_OFFLOAD` flips too, which is why the flip order pairs them in one window (`ENABLE_SYNC_DB_OFFLOAD` + ST1). **Flag OFF:** byte-identical on every response body and stored row: the same calls in the same order on the same threads, no pool built, no new log line, the base response literals in base key order (pinned by `tests/test_s81_st1_usage_path_offloop.py` r08 / r09 / r12 / r13 / r16 / r23 / r25b / r27 / r35 with the full configuration and a call / thread recorder, and the W4-13 45-scenario ledger r34); `consume_comparison_credit`, `_refund_credit_sync`, every quota key helper, the two gathered helpers and the S77 reader / pool functions are AST-digest pinned. Fix round 1 pins (every ON-path case sets the flag inside the row; r39[direct_cancelled] drives `run_redis_offloop_pool` directly and sets no flag, the dispatcher being flag-independent): r36 the ON write follows the FY19 / error persist guard exactly as OFF, r37 a compute failure is not a dispatch failure, r38 every ON write task sits in the drain registry `_BACKGROUND_TASKS` as `reviews.l1_write`, r39 a failed durable pool job is retrieved quietly, r40 a cancelled previous write does not drop the next, r41 a snapshot failure skips the write and keeps the response, r42 / r42b the GETs-first order and the GT-6 close under it. No new response key, no new error code; `/health` gains nothing (its existing `redis_offloop_pool` key appears once the pool exists, as under S77). **Stated limits:** (1) the reviews L2-promotion write stays inline (RO17 reorder class); (2) the auth half of `/usage/status` (revocation GET + `auth.get_user`) and the reviews L2 read stay on the loop until `ENABLE_ASYNC_REDIS_OFFLOAD` / `ENABLE_SYNC_DB_OFFLOAD`; (3) ONE FIFO pool shared with the S77 catalog GETs: the usage GETs and the reviews writes queue behind `shopify_catalog` GETs under cold-compare load (S77 measured 0.5-3.4 s FIFO waits for small reads), so the loop is freed but `/usage/status` latency may not drop under load, and a reviews L1 write can land seconds after the response (a lookup inside that window misses L1 and falls to L2); (4) no new timeout: a stalled Upstash call parks a pool thread for the client's own timeout while `ENABLE_UPSTASH_BOUNDED_TRANSPORT` is held; (5) `check_usage_allowed` ON reads both counters on the lifetime-free path (two extra GETs; no production caller); (6) fail-open on a Redis outage unchanged (zero counts); (7) a dispatch failure falls back to the inline base path, which blocks the loop for that call, and an asynchronous failure inside the gather can repeat at most one read set (duplicate GETs and Supabase reads and, with a past `referral_bonus_reset_at`, a second reset UPDATE, reachable only with `ENABLE_SYNC_DB_OFFLOAD` ON, since with it OFF the tier task runs its select and UPDATE in its first step, before any pool failure can surface: idempotent, the counter is display-only); (8) two concurrent first reads after a past `reset_at` can both issue the reset UPDATE when `ENABLE_SYNC_DB_OFFLOAD` is ON: a property of that flag (base included), not of ST1 (measured by both adversaries with a stateful fake row and the real `_maybe_reset_referral_bonus`: 1 / 1 UPDATE with sync offload OFF and 2 / 2 with it ON, ST1 off / on; the 2-vs-1 figure belonged to the dropped `run_db_offloop` design; display-only counter); (9) the date keys are computed before the reads, so a call straddling UTC midnight can show the old day's counter (display only; S81-09 owns the Riyadh key change and edits the one compute body); (10) no latency claim without the MS1 baseline (the unit tests prove loop freedom with fakes only); (11) the `[REDIS-OFFLOOP] pool built` line can now come from an ST1 dispatch; (12) the timing rows r06 / r07 / r31 / r31b / r42 carry fixed bounds: a red on a slow CI runner is fixed by widening the bound in a test-only commit, never by a code change (GT-5); (13) never export the flag in a local `.env`: 9 existing nodes encode the inline reviews L1 calls and go red with it exported (measured at the GREEN commit and again at the fix-round commit: six in `tests/test_s77_redis_offloop.py` (`test_s6_reviews_l1_read_placement[off]`, `test_s6_reviews_l2_promotion_set_stays_inline[off]`, `test_s6_post_extraction_write_stays_inline_on`, `test_parity_s6[nocache]`, `test_off_call_shapes_exact[S6]`, `test_off_no_new_suspension_on_hit_paths[s6_l1_hit-off]`), `tests/test_review_source_consult.py::test_get_reviews_persists_extraction_before_consult` and `::test_get_reviews_extraction_persisted_even_if_consult_cancels`, and `tests/test_youtube_reviews_race.py::test_get_reviews_persists_extraction_before_youtube_consult`); CI runs it unset, and the unit file's autouse fixture deletes the flag before every node, so the ON evidence in CI is the rows that set the flag themselves; (14) cross-request L1 write order is not serialised: the writes are chained per request only, so for two concurrent requests on the same reviews key the pool FIFO decides which request lands last (the last snapshot of each request wins per key; both are full extractions of the same product). **Precondition:** none to flip; ST1 flips after approval in the `ENABLE_SYNC_DB_OFFLOAD` + ST1 window of the flip order, after MS1 is recorded, with the owner's explicit yes. **Canary:** `/health loop_lag_max_60s_ms` not worse than before the flip (no `/health` p95 expectation: the route is mostly a victim of other blockers), `usage_status` p50 / p95 and the Home / Paywall mount timings (Sentry), `redis_offloop_pool.queued` between compares (RO20), the `[USAGE-OFFLOOP]` WARNING count (expect 0; the tag counts the usage dispatch failures (`dispatch failed fn=<fn>`) AND the reviews L1 write failures (`reviews L1 write dispatch failed type=<Type>`: a deepcopy snapshot failure or an exception inside the pool write, as r41 and r39[reviews_write] pin; `set_cached` swallows its own errors, so the in-pool case is near-unreachable in production); a usage compute-body failure is not counted since fix round 1), `[REDIS-OFFLOOP] pool built` once per process, the reviews-leg timeout count, the Upstash daily command count (unchanged expected). **Rollback:** unset the variable (per call, immediate).

**SESSION 81 flag (S81-05 REQUEST-CONTEXT, 2026-10-23; default OFF, read PER CALL; a new name, so unset in production by construction (Railway not read); PLAN_S81 unit S81-05 (M1, lane B); spec `.qa-s80/S81-05_UNIT_SPEC.md` of the unit worktree with its adversarial review (RC1-RC6, B1, C1-C15, K1-K5), the rulings `specs/RULINGS_S81_05.md` (R-S05-1..R-S05-11) and the RED gate `specs/GATE_S81_05.md` (GC-1..GC-10) and the fix-round rulings `specs/RULINGS_FIX1_S81_05.md` (F1-1..F1-11) in `docs/investigations/2026-10-22-session-81-state/`):**
- `ENABLE_REQUEST_CONTEXT_HEADERS` (S81-05, `app/utils/request_context.py::request_context_headers_enabled`, a stdlib-only leaf; read once per ASGI call of any scope type by `app/middleware/request_context.py::RequestContextMiddleware` (the flag read is the first statement), a pure-ASGI class registered UNCONDITIONALLY as the INNERMOST user middleware in `app/main.py`, just before the CORS add, so RequestID / SecurityHeaders / ErrorHandler / SlowAPIASGI (under `ENABLE_DEFAULT_RATE_LIMITS`) / CORS run outside the ContextVar scope and cannot observe the values, while the `@limiter.limit` decorators, the exception handlers, the dependencies and the routes run inside it) + credential NAME `DEVICE_KEY_PEPPER` (read per call by `device_key`; in `tests/_env_safety.CREDENTIALS_UNSET`, so a test sets it only after importing `app.main`). **Effect ON:** for an HTTP request (lifespan and websocket scopes pass through unbound) three request headers are parsed into ContextVars and reset in `finally` when the request returns or raises: `X-AI-Consent-Version` -> `consent_version()` (an int 0..999 written as 1-3 ASCII digits: no sign, no space, leading zeros allowed; S81-08 sends `String(AI_CONSENT_VERSION)`), `X-Device-Fingerprint` -> `device_fingerprint()` (the existing `^[a-f0-9]{64}$`, matched in full; the compiled object now lives in the leaf as `DEVICE_FINGERPRINT_RE` and `auth_routes` re-imports it under its old name `_DEVICE_FINGERPRINT_RE`, so the register / social reader is unchanged), `X-Compare-Request-Id` -> `compare_request_id()` (a canonical uuid4 with the RFC variant; upper or mixed case is accepted and normalised to the canonical lower-case form); the FIRST occurrence of each header decides and later duplicates are ignored; a missing, empty, malformed or non-ASCII first occurrence reads `None`: never a 400, never logged, never a changed byte. `header_state(name)` returns the constant `"absent"` (not sent), `"malformed"` (sent, but empty or invalid: the class a forged-header metric counts) or `"ok"` per header, never a value; it takes the leaf's bytes constants `HEADER_CONSENT_VERSION` / `HEADER_DEVICE_FINGERPRINT` / `HEADER_COMPARE_REQUEST_ID` (compared as exact bytes: ASGI servers deliver lower-case header names) or the header name as a str (case-insensitive), and reads `"absent"` for an unknown name and outside a bound request; only the three constants are ever bound. The bind is TOTAL and ATOMIC: every header is parsed into locals and the ContextVars (values and states) are set only after every parse finished; if anything raises, every value is `None` and every state `"malformed"`; the reset is total too (a failing reset never replaces the request's own exception). `device_key(fp)` = HMAC-SHA256(the stripped pepper, fp) as 64 lower-case hex; fail closed: an invalid fingerprint returns `None` without reading the pepper, an unset or blank pepper, or one that cannot be UTF-8 encoded (the `UnicodeError` is caught), returns `None` plus ONE WARNING per process `[REQUEST-CONTEXT] DEVICE_KEY_PEPPER unset or blank; device keys disabled (fail closed)` (the same line for both; never the value, never the exception text), never a fallback key. **No consumer yet** (pinned by `tests/test_s81_s05_request_context.py::test_s05_p01_no_consumer_in_app`): S81-06 consent routing reads `consent_version()`, S81-09 device quota reads `device_fingerprint()` / `header_state()` and calls `device_key()`, S81-22 compare runs reads `compare_request_id()`; each of them lists P1 as its deliberate pin change; until then the flag is inert. **Flag OFF:** one `os.getenv` per ASGI call, no scope key read, `bind_from_headers` / `reset_request_context` never called, the identical `scope` / `receive` / `send` objects downstream, every accessor `None` and every state `"absent"`; flag OFF and ON are byte-identical on every response by construction (the same scope / receive / send objects, r22 / r23); measured on the 401 / 404 envelopes in four states (P4: status, body and headers against base literals) and on 12 request shapes by the correctness adversary (the unknown-route 404 is FastAPI's default `{"detail":"Not Found"}`, not the unified envelope: pre-existing, follow-up S81-05b); the only structural change is one more `app.user_middleware` entry (`tests/test_retro_w1_9.py` `EXPECTED_STACK`, the one deliberate pin change, 7 nodes); no route parameter, schema, response key or OpenAPI change (the T23 fixture is not re-recorded); CORS `allow_headers` unchanged. **Order dependency (a precondition for every consumer flag):** every consumer reads `None` while this flag is OFF, so `ENABLE_REQUEST_CONTEXT_HEADERS` goes ON before any consumer flag (flipping S81-06 first routes every request as consent `None`; flipping S81-09 first makes every device account-only). **ContextVar rule for consumers:** read the accessors on the request path BEFORE any offload: `asyncio.to_thread`, Starlette's threadpool (sync routes and dependencies, sync streaming generators), `app/utils/redis_offloop.py` and the price-parse pool copy the context, and a task created inside the request keeps the request's values after it ends (a snapshot taken at its creation); `loop.run_in_executor` with the installed default executor (`app/utils/executor.py`) does NOT copy the context and reads `None` (r26 pins the default-executor case; the qaren-worker pool is a plain ThreadPoolExecutor with the same contract (read)). **Device-fingerprint transition (R-S05-1):** the plan's "dual-hash transition map" (`legacy_hash` / `device_lookup_keys`) was dropped (pinned by p08): the server stores the client's fingerprint VALUE verbatim (the register and social sign-in rows, the referral SAME_DEVICE check) and the S81-08 OTA changes that value, so no server-side hash bridges it; the transition is dual-VALUE and belongs to S81-08 + S81-09: for a window the client sends its old composition in a second header `X-Device-Fingerprint-Legacy` (the same 64-hex shape; S81-08's contract), which this unit does not parse. **Stated limits:** (1) build 3 sends `X-Device-Fingerprint` as a header only on register and the two social sign-ins (`authService.ts`), share carries the hash in the JSON body (`/api/v1/referrals/share`, not read by the middleware), and it never sends the other two headers, so every accessor reads `None` on compare, camera, usage and share requests until the S81-08 OTA; (2) the consent version is the client's claim (S81-06 treats it as a claim, not proof); (3) the fingerprint is client-generated and only format-checked (a script can send a fresh value per request; a device cap built on it bounds honest clients only), and both the S81-08 composition change and a pepper rotation restart every device key; (4) the innermost position means a CORS preflight never binds (no metered work) and the default-limit 429 of `SlowAPIASGIMiddleware` is produced outside the scope, so a future device-keyed limiter must be a decorator or a consumer inside the route: never the default-limit (`SlowAPIASGIMiddleware`) key_func, which runs outside the ContextVar scope; a per-route `@limiter.limit` key_func runs inside it and may read these values (measured); (5) CORS `allow_headers` does not list the three headers (native clients do not preflight; an Expo web build would be refused); (6) no minimum pepper length is enforced; (7) the leaf's full match rejects the trailing-newline fingerprint that the `auth_routes` / `usage_service` `.match` readers accept, which no HTTP/1.1 parser (httptools on uvloop in production) can deliver, so the readers agree on every reachable value; (8) no DB change and no privacy text change here (the device counters and their retention move in S81-12 LEGAL-V3); (9) one `os.getenv` per ASGI call of any scope type in both flag states (the flag read is the first statement; not timed). **Owner steps (names only):** set `DEVICE_KEY_PEPPER` on `web` (a random text value of at least 64 hex characters, i.e. 32 bytes of entropy; never in the repo, chat or a ledger) BEFORE the flag flips; flip `ENABLE_REQUEST_CONTEXT_HEADERS` in the post-approval window "ledger, breaker, request context ON" of the PLAN_S81 flip order, after the S81-08 OTA has propagated, in its own canary window; record both as dated ledger lines (names only). **Canary:** none needed for bytes (the flag is inert until a consumer flips); the WARNING can fire only once a consumer calls `device_key` (S81-09); until then confirm `DEVICE_KEY_PEPPER` by NAME on `web` before that consumer flips. **Rollback:** unset the variable (read per ASGI call, immediate).

**SESSION 81 flag (S81-04 SPEND-BREAKER, 2026-10-23; default OFF, read PER CALL; a new name, so unset in production by construction (Railway not read); PLAN_S81 M1 unit S81-04 (lane B); spec `.qa-s80/S81-04_UNIT_SPEC.md` of the unit worktree with its adversarial review (B1-B2, corrections 1-16), the rulings `specs/RULINGS_S81_04.md` (R-S04-1..R-S04-11) and the RED gate `specs/GATE_S81_04.md` (GC-1..GC-14) in `docs/investigations/2026-10-22-session-81-state/`):**
- `ENABLE_LLM_SPEND_BREAKER` (S81-04, `app/services/llm_spend_breaker.py::spend_breaker_enabled`, read PER CALL as the first statement of every gated branch: `api_budget_service.guarded_llm_create`, the two compare entries `structured_comparison_service.compare_from_text` / `compare_from_text_streaming` and the admin costs block) + knobs, each read per call, unset / blank / garbage / inf / nan / <= 0 -> the default, none a secret: `LLM_SPEND_CAP_LIVE_USD` (3.00), `LLM_SPEND_CAP_ESCALATION_USD` (1.00), `LLM_SPEND_CAP_SEARCH_USD` (0.50), `LLM_SPEND_CAP_OFFCLOCK_USD` (2.00) USD per Asia/Riyadh day (the owner's open ruling, bracketed defaults), `LLM_SPEND_ENTRY_RESERVE_USD` (0.10), `LLM_SPEND_MEMO_SECONDS` (5.0), `LLM_SPEND_REDIS_TIMEOUT_SECONDS` (0.5) and `LLM_SPEND_CHOKEPOINT_LANE` (default `live`; stripped and lower-cased, anything outside live / escalation / search / offclock -> live); the pool size is a constant (4 workers, no knob). **What it is:** a LAST-RESORT cap, not an abuse control (R-S04-8 / R-S04-11; the owner's ruling: about 140 compares a day at 3 USD): one exhausted `live` lane refuses every compare of every user until 00:00 Asia/Riyadh; the abuse controls are S81-09 DEVICE-QUOTA and S81-24 AUTH-THROTTLE, which the flip order places after the breaker. **Effect ON:** every OpenAI chat completion through `guarded_llm_create` is ADMITTED on the chokepoint lane BEFORE the W1-3 breaker admission (a spend refusal never holds the half-open probe) from an UPPER BOUND priced by the S81-03 `llm_pricing.price_call`: the UTF-8 bytes of every message as tokens + 4 per message + 16 per call + the JSON bytes of `response_format` / `tools`, an image part per MODEL whatever its data-URL size (R-S04-9 as refined by fix-round ruling F1-7): 1,445 tokens for an OpenAI id priced at the 4o rate row (85 + 170 x 8), `OPENAI_MINI_IMAGE_TOKENS_UPPER` = 49,000 for every other OpenAI id (the gpt-4o-mini rows, the default vision model: 2,833 base + 5,667 per tile at the 8 tiles of the same 768 x 2048 resize the 4o bound uses = 48,169, rounded up (fix-round ruling F2-3), UNVERIFIED by a web read and marked for S81-07; an unpriced or missing id), 4,784 on Anthropic and the largest of the three for an unknown provider, `max_tokens` / `max_completion_tokens` or 16,384 (`LLM_SPEND_FALLBACK_OUTPUT_TOKENS`); an unpriced model id is bounded at the most expensive row of its provider (the 4o row on OpenAI, the Sonnet 5.5 row otherwise, R-S04-3) and a garbage or zero bound at that rate for 16,384 output tokens (GC-3, F1-5: never a zero-cost admit). spent + bound > cap (strict) raises the existing `LLMUnavailableError` before any dispatch (one WARNING `[SPEND] lane=<lane> cap reached (spent_cents=<n> cap_cents=<n> bound_cents=<n>) -- dispatch refused`, ints only). A SERVED response is RECORDED non-awaited (a durable `fire_and_forget` task `llm_spend.record`, covered by the shutdown drain) with ONE atomic Lua EVAL (`INCRBYFLOAT` + `EXPIREAT` the next Riyadh midnight + 1 h) of its list price, or of the admitted bound when the model is unpriced or the response carries no usage (never zero, GC-4); a raising or cancelled create records nothing; a record is never windowed and always attempts its EVAL, and a record whose EVAL fails keeps its delta in this process's memo for the rest of its Riyadh day (the overcount-safe direction) and counts in `llm_spend_fail_open` at most once per lane per memo window, opening no read window (F2-2). The lane total lives in Upstash under `llm_spend:<lane>:<YYYY-MM-DD>` (the Riyadh day, UTC+3 fixed) and is memoised per process for `LLM_SPEND_MEMO_SECONDS` together with this process's in-flight record deltas (R-S04-1): a fresh memo costs no Redis command, a due read is ONE GET per lane shared by every concurrent reader of that lane (single flight, F1-4b); within one Riyadh day a GET submitted before a record of that day landed keeps the larger of its total and that record's EVAL total (F1-2: each in-flight delta is settled exactly once on landing, the base never moves down on an EVAL), and an observation of an EARLIER Riyadh day (a previous-day settle, a pre-midnight record or GET landing after midnight) never replaces the memo entry of a later day, so the `/health` `day` never reverts; an EVAL total below 0 is observed as 0, so the admin `spent_usd` is never negative (F2-1). Every Redis command runs on the dedicated `llm-spend` pool (4 workers; never the loop, never the `qaren-worker` default executor, never the `redis-offloop` pool) bounded by `LLM_SPEND_REDIS_TIMEOUT_SECONDS` (async callers via `asyncio.wait_for`, sync callers via `future.result(timeout)`; R-S04-2), and with no Redis client nothing is submitted at all. Both compare entries return the existing `LLM_UNAVAILABLE` envelope (REST coded 503 + refund, SSE `error` + refund; no history row, no lifetime bump, R-A3) BEFORE any paid provider when spent + `LLM_SPEND_ENTRY_RESERVE_USD` >= cap on the chokepoint lane (`LLM_SPEND_CHOKEPOINT_LANE`, live by default: the SAME lane the chokepoint charges, F1-6) (R-S04-4; one WARNING `[SPEND] lane=<lane> cap reached (...) -- short-circuiting the compare BEFORE the provider fan-out`, no query text). `/health` gains `llm_spend` {lane: {exhausted, day}} (`exhausted` is the SAME predicate as the entry check, one helper `lane_exhausted`, GC-6, over this process's view with its in-flight record deltas, on the current Riyadh day only: an entry of an earlier day reads false, F2-1) and the counters `llm_spend_fail_open` and `llm_spend_late_records` (F1-4a: shielded records that landed after their bound), all present only once the breaker ran in this process (a fail-open counts, GC-5), never an amount; the amounts (`spent_usd`, `cap_usd`, `day`, `memo_age_seconds` per observed lane) are the additive `spend_breaker` object of GET `/api/v1/admin/costs` (X-Admin-Key), written only ON (R-S04-7). The leaf also carries `reserve(lane, ub)` (ONE Lua check-and-increment returning a `ReserveTicket` (lane, bound_usd, the Asia/Riyadh day of the reserve and that day's EXPIREAT) when the bound is held, None on a fail-open) and `settle(ticket, actual)` (one INCRBYFLOAT of actual - bound on THE TICKET'S day with THE TICKET'S EXPIREAT, never the settle-time day, so a batch reserved before 00:00 Riyadh and settled after it charges the reserve day and no key goes negative; a 0 actual releases the whole bound; a non-ticket is a ValueError) for the batch / search / cron submitters (R-S04-5, fix-round ruling F1-1): THE TICKET IS THE BINDING CONTRACT for S81-16 / S81-21 / S81-22, stated with the rest of the contract every later dispatch follows in the leaf docstring (S81-06 Anthropic calls, S81-16 / S81-22 batches and crons on `offclock`, S81-21 web_search on `search`, the Sonnet escalation on `escalation`). A negative stored total (only a pre-fix process, settling on the settle-time day, wrote one) reads as 0 with one rate-limited WARNING per key, `[SPEND] stored spend negative (key=<key>) -- read as 0`. Not a `PROVIDER_CONFIGS` provider (R-S03-9). **Flag OFF:** byte-identical on every response body and stored row: no admit, no record, no Lua, no thread hop, no task, no health key, no admin key in a process where the flag was never ON (pinned by `tests/test_s81_s04_spend_breaker.py` P01-P09 under the full configuration: every knob set, a fake Upstash seeded over cap on every lane, call / submit / task recorders; by the FY25 nine-digest node with the flag in `UNIT_ENV` and by the W4-13 flag-OFF ledger); ON with headroom changes no response field either (P08). After a mid-process rollback `/health` keeps the `llm_spend*` keys until the next restart (the snapshot reads whether the breaker ran in this process, `_STATE['ran']`, never the flag: the RO18 precedent). **Deliberate pin changes:** the K1 hash of `guarded_llm_create` in `tests/test_s81_s03_llm_ledger_v2.py` (E1, GC-8) and the FY25 `UNIT_ENV` entry (E2); the U3c pins stay untouched (two `create(**kwargs)`, `store=False`). **Stated limits:** (1) FAIL-OPEN: no Redis client, an error, a command slower than the timeout or a bad stored value fails open (one `[SPEND] breaker fail-open (<Type>) -- admitting and not recording` WARNING per process per minute and the `llm_spend_fail_open` counter): a read admits; a read failure or timeout opens a per-lane FAIL-OPEN WINDOW as long as `LLM_SPEND_MEMO_SECONDS`, inside which admit and the entry check fail open WITHOUT a Redis hop (counted once per window, F1-4c), so a stalled Upstash costs at most one `LLM_SPEND_REDIS_TIMEOUT_SECONDS` wait per lane per window; the window gates READS only (fix-round ruling F2-2): `record_soon` / `record` always attempt their shielded, bounded EVAL, a record whose EVAL fails keeps its delta in this process's memo for the rest of its Riyadh day and counts at most once per lane per window, and a record that lands after its bound counts late, so no record is skipped on the request path (measured 18/18 and 47/47 sent); a failed EVAL's spend stays in THIS process's view only (never Upstash, another replica, the warmer or this process after a restart), and the stall cost on the request path is read latency only (limit 17 names the record backlog under a hung transport; `record_soon` never waits; a sync `record`, which no request-path caller uses, waits up to the bound, and one still QUEUED at its bound is cancelled and its delta settled, F1-3: the one record that can be dropped) (before the window, measured on the GREEN bytes with a stalled fake by both adversaries: every due read waited the full bound, 3 admits + 1 entry check took 2.02 s, 12 admits 6.06 s); during an Upstash outage there is NO code-side bound and the Console workspace limit is the outer bound; the herd thresholds on a HEALTHY Upstash were measured by the engineering adversary on the GREEN bytes (a fake at 0.18 s per command, the 0.5 s default bound, 4 workers): with no single flight k concurrent stale-memo admits issued k GETs (k = 4: 0.187 s each; k = 8: max 0.36 s; k = 12: 4 failed open; k = 16: 8 failed open), and record EVALs share the FIFO with reads (4 queued: the due read waited 0.406 s; 8: the read failed open at 0.516 s; 12: the read and 4 records hit their bound, the records still landing); since fix round 1 the readers of one lane share ONE GET, a shielded record that lands after its bound is counted in `llm_spend_late_records` with one INFO `[SPEND] late record landed -- recorded after its bound expired` (never as a fail-open), and 8 or more queued EVALs (the read is the 9th job) can still push a due read past its bound at that round trip (measured by the round-1 re-verifier on the fix bytes: the read failed open at 0.515 s; the read then opens the window, the records inside it are still sent; the production Railway-to-Upstash RTT is unmeasured); (2) the bound over-estimates text (bytes as tokens, the max output), so a lane refuses a call a little BEFORE the true spend reaches the cap; (3) admit reads, it does not reserve: concurrent in-flight calls can overshoot a cap by their own bounds; another process's spend is seen at each of this process's own record EVALs (an EVAL returns the global total) and at each GET, and a settle decrease is seen only at a GET submitted after it (an overcount, the safe direction); the record lands after the response, so a lane can end a day slightly above its cap; (4) a call that raises or is cancelled records nothing (a provider may still bill a timed-out request); (5) a compare that starts under the entry reserve and crosses the cap mid-way has its later calls refused at the chokepoint: such a refusal of the verdict becomes a degraded, BILLED 200 through `generate_comparison`'s broad except (`comparison.error`, the template winner; metered like any degraded result), so only the ENTRY refusal is the refunded non-delivery (S81-06's R-S06-2 makes a mid-compare refusal a non-delivery under a role flag later); URL mode and the camera have no entry check (S81-04b; the camera vision refusal is already the coded 503); (6) an exhausted lane refuses every call of that lane until 00:00 Asia/Riyadh: the only override is raising the cap name on web (read per call); (7) OpenAI calls count at the S81-03 list price, not the data-sharing-discounted bill; the per-model image bound is the published vision rule at 8 tiles, the gpt-4o-mini figure (49,000, F2-3) UNVERIFIED by a web read (S81-07): over-bounding only refuses earlier near the cap, and that holds only at this constant (a mini rule above 49,000 tokens per image would under-bound the admit); the record uses the served usage; (8) each due read adds one Upstash round trip (163-203 ms measured with TLS) of wall time to the admitted call (memo hits add none; concurrent readers of a lane share it), and the REST entry read sits before the `STREAM_HARD_CAP_SECONDS` cap (bounded by the timeout); (9) every refusal reaches the callers' existing exc_info ERROR logs (`extraction_service` parse and verdict, `image_routes` vision, `url_extraction_service`): Sentry events from the moment a lane is exhausted (follow-up S81-04c downgrades them for `LLMUnavailableError`); (10) `/health` and the admin view show what THIS process last read or wrote, its in-flight record deltas included (another replica's or the warmer's spend, and a record in flight elsewhere, appear once this process reads the key or records: one in-flight record of staleness, corrected by the next read or landing, F2-5); (11) the chokepoint lane is per process; when a script's `asyncio.run` exits, a pending record task is cancelled but its EVAL is already submitted and shielded, so interpreter exit WAITS for the queued records on the non-daemon `llm-spend` threads (measured by the engineering adversary: the warmer's last records land; exit blocks for as long as Upstash takes, and the SDK transport has no timeout while `ENABLE_UPSTASH_BOUNDED_TRANSPORT` is held); such a cancelled task whose EVAL lands afterwards is counted in `llm_spend_late_records` although it was not slow (scripts only; web's loop lives for the process, F2-6); (12) these are the repo's first Lua scripts: their acceptance by Upstash's REST EVAL is measured only at the canary; (13) the Riyadh day helper is module-local (S81-09 may move it); (14) a fail-open `reserve` may still have landed (a timeout), so the submitter records instead of settling: an overcount, never an undercount; a key a pre-fix settle drove negative stays negative for the RESERVE Lua until it expires (the read clamp covers the GET paths only); (15) never set the flag in a local `.env` or shell: with it exported, 34 unedited pins go red (measured over the 24-file pin set at the GREEN commit and again at the fix-round-1 commit, the same 34 nodes): 6 `tests/test_retro_w0_4_efg.py::test_w04g_health_*` and 7 `tests/test_s77_redis_offloop.py::test_health_*` /health superset pins (the test tier has no Redis client, so every chokepoint call is a counted fail-open and `/health` carries the `llm_spend*` keys for the rest of the process) and 21 `tests/test_s81_s03_llm_ledger_v2.py` flag-OFF recorder pins (b4 / b4s / b5 / d1 / d3 / d5b / d6b / i2 / i6: the breaker prices its bounds through `llm_pricing` whatever `ENABLE_LLM_LEDGER_V2` says); CI runs it unset (R-S04-8: the flag joins no clear-list but FY25's `UNIT_ENV`); (16) two scripts dispatch chat completions outside `guarded_llm_create` and are NOT capped: `scripts/seed_spec_spine.py` and `scripts/shadow_experiments.py`; (17) the measured F2-2 trade-off (the fix-2 re-verifier, hermetic fakes): an EVAL that Upstash APPLIED but whose response raised keeps its delta as well, so that spend counts twice in this process for the rest of the Riyadh day (cap 1.0, one admit + record of 0.1 every 6 s: Upstash held 0.5, this process's view 1.0, refused from the 6th call): the breaker refuses EARLY on a degraded Upstash (the overcount-safe direction; a canary line: `[SPEND] ... dispatch refused` while `llm_spend_fail_open` climbs and the admin `spent_usd` runs ahead of the traffic); and a hung transport (no SDK timeout while `ENABLE_UPSTASH_BOUNDED_TRANSPORT` is held) grows the `llm-spend` FIFO by one job per record until the backlog drains (measured: 44 jobs queued after 6 s at about 8 records/s, all 47 landed late on release) while the reads queue behind it and keep failing open. **Activation precondition:** with OpenAI still serving, check the daily compare volume x about 0.021 USD (list price per compare, runs/critic.json) against `LLM_SPEND_CAP_LIVE_USD`, and confirm every `OPENAI_MODEL_*` role id resolves to a priced row; set the cap names on web first if a default would refuse real traffic. **Owner steps (names only):** (a) nothing to set for the defaults; any of the eight knob names on web only if a default is wrong; (b) on `price-warmer` set `LLM_SPEND_CHOKEPOINT_LANE=offclock` BEFORE the flag (R-S04-6), or never set the flag there; (c) keep the Console workspace limit (decision 8) as the outer bound; (d) flip after approval in the plan's flip order ("ledger, breaker, request context ON"), BEFORE any Anthropic flag (R-S03-9). **Canary (the owner's explicit yes, one window):** `/admin/costs` `spend_breaker.live.spent_usd` moving with traffic and its `day` rolling at 00:00 Riyadh, `/health llm_spend.live.exhausted` false, `llm_spend_fail_open` flat after warm-up (`llm_spend_late_records` is the slow-but-alive Upstash counter), the `[SPEND] ... dispatch refused` / `short-circuiting the compare` / `breaker fail-open` / `late record landed` counts by lane, the `LLM_UNAVAILABLE` 503 count, one `[SPEND] pool built workers=4` line per process, `/health loop_lag_max_60s_ms` unchanged, client wall time per compare. **Rollback:** unset the variable (per call, immediate for new calls; `/health` keeps the `llm_spend*` keys until the next restart); the keys expire by themselves.

**SESSION 81 unit (S81-14 KB-SCHEMA, 2026-10-24; NO flag and no call site: a pure leaf with zero importers and two migrations WRITTEN, NOT APPLIED; spec `.qa-s80/S81-14_UNIT_SPEC.md` of the unit worktree with its adversarial review (BL1-BL4, C1-C19), the rulings `specs/RULINGS_S81_14.md` (R-S14-1..R-S14-16), the RED gate `specs/GATE_S81_14.md` (GC-1..GC-10; GC-4 amended by F1-3) and the fix-round rulings `specs/RULINGS_FIX1_S81_14.md` (F1-1..F1-12) and `specs/RULINGS_FIX2_S81_14.md` (F2-1..F2-10) in `docs/investigations/2026-10-22-session-81-state/`):**
- **What lands (inert):** `migrations/045_kb_core.sql` + `migrations/rollback/045_kb_core.sql`: ten service-role tables (`kb_merchants`, `kb_products`, `kb_variants`, `kb_offers`, `kb_images`, `kb_specs`, `kb_reviews`, `kb_gaps`, `kb_feedback`, `kb_coverage_daily`), the view `kb_offers_latest` (security_invoker), five SECURITY INVOKER functions with a pinned search_path (`kb_gtin_check_digit_ok(text)`, the trigger function `kb_set_updated_at()`, `kb_prune_offers(integer, integer)`, `kb_prune_feedback(integer)`, `kb_product_card(text, text DEFAULT 'en')`) and three BEFORE UPDATE triggers that maintain `updated_at` on kb_merchants / kb_products / kb_variants (the builders emit `updated_at` too: a belt); RLS on, no policy, REVOKE ALL from PUBLIC, anon, authenticated AND service_role, then one GRANT to service_role (tables SELECT / INSERT / UPDATE / DELETE, the view SELECT, the functions EXECUTE: the 050 form); 045 refuses PostgreSQL below 15. `migrations/047_012_revoke.sql` + rollback: the eight 012 policies on `product_specs` / `product_prices` / `product_reviews` dropped BY NAME, RLS kept on, every privilege revoked from PUBLIC, anon and authenticated, service_role re-granted SELECT / INSERT / UPDATE / DELETE (every 012 reader and writer uses the service-role client, pinned by P3; the text_routes cache flush DELETEs product_prices). `app/services/kb_service.py`: a pure leaf (its own code does no I/O, no log, no clock, no env read, no flag; never raises) holding the identity helpers, the deny helpers, `canonical_offer_url`, `gtin_check_digit_ok` and one row builder per table whose keys equal the 045 columns; every builder refuses a NUL character or a lone surrogate in any text or jsonb value, keys included (PostgreSQL 22P05 / 22P02; F1-8; S81-17's per-row validation inherits the rule). Import hazard (F1-11): importing the leaf imports price_service, which imports extraction_service, which calls `load_dotenv(override=True)` at import, so every S81-15..S81-22 script that imports it inherits the dotenv-override hazard P8 pins (set a flag only after the first import); and the function-local `from app.services import source_router` in the registry validator runs source_router's module body on the first merchant build: it reads `ENABLE_BH_GCC_CATALOG_SOURCES`, reads `data/bh_gcc_sources.json` when that flag is ON (production) and imports search_descriptor_service (F2-8; "no env read" covers the leaf's own code only). **`.sqlfluff` gains `large_file_skip_byte_limit = 0`** (R-S14-12: sqlfluff 4.3.0 silently skips a file above the 20,000-byte default with a WARNING and exit 0, and 045 is 25,858 bytes; 0 lints every file and the 037 pin accepts it). Nothing in app/ or scripts/ imports the leaf or names a kb_* relation (P1 / P2: S81-15, S81-17 and S81-18 list them as deliberate pin changes).
- **Identity (R-S14-3, R-S14-5, R-S14-9, R-S14-14, GC-1, F1-1):** a JUICE `kb_products.id` = `spec_spine_service.spine_key(brand, name, variant)` minus its ml field (brand axis + identity tokens + concentration: EDP and EDT are different juices; PLAN_S81's "variant on concentration + ml" wording is superseded for the id), a SKU `kb_variants.id` = the juice id + `|<ml>` (`kb_variant_key` validates the passed concentration against the id; a size of 0 or above 5000 ml maps to `-`, so for 5001..9999 ml it diverges from spine_key on purpose); no id (None) for an empty identity (a one-letter name such as YSL "Y"), a placeholder (`result_gate.product_is_placeholder`, read at call time: the identity (brand + name deduplicated) OR the name alone, so ("Dior", "Unknown"), ("Dior", "Product") and ("Lattafa", "Unknown") mint none; R-A5), a placeholder BRAND (F2-5: `result_gate.is_placeholder_identity` on the brand, also with the generic word "brand" dropped, so ("Unknown", "Sauvage"), ("N/A", "Sauvage"), ("Unknown Brand", "Sauvage") and ("Product", "Sauvage") mint none; the brand's words come from the house `result_gate.norm` split, so "Unknown-Brand", "Unknown Brand." and "Unknown_Brand" mint none too, RB-5 row r22d2) or a non-str input; the writers' contract (S81-16 / S81-17): they never pass a placeholder brand or name. The ids inherit the price path's per-call flag reads, so seeding and serving must run under the same flag state (production: `ENABLE_EXACT_PRICE_GATE` ON, `ENABLE_NORMALIZE_WORDS_EMPTY_FIX` OFF; pinned literals) and a flip MAY re-mint ids (the correctness adversary measured no corpus id change under either flag over 41 titles).
- **Offers (R-S14-1, GC-2, F1-4):** a `market` column (the storefront's country in the registry's own country vocabulary, BH, UAE, KSA, QA, OM, KW, not ISO 3166; S81-17 maps it from the registry country); `captured_at` = the first observation of one exact (variant, merchant, market, observed_currency, amount, list_vs_sale) row and never moves; the writer's re-observation upserts on `kb_offers_observation` and sets only `last_seen, in_stock, evidence_rung, canonical_url` (the leaf constants `KB_OFFER_OBSERVATION_KEY` / `KB_OFFER_UPSERT_SET`, which S81-17's migration 051 consumes); a changed amount is a new row; the view, the card and the prune rank `last_seen DESC, (list_vs_sale = 'sale') DESC, captured_at DESC, id DESC` per (variant, merchant, market, observed_currency) and `idx_kb_offers_latest` carries the same key in the same position (F2-1: among the rows last seen at the same instant the sale wins, then the newest capture, so a list price that changed during an ongoing sale never outranks the still-observed sale and the prune keeps the sale; the sale is what the shopper pays; S81-17's ingest writes a list and a sale price as two rows of one observation; RB-7, the S81-17 contract its brief inherits: one observation = ONE shared last_seen value for every row S81-17 writes; the list and sale rows of one observation carry the same last_seen, else the later-stamped row wins whatever its kind) and the prune ages rows by last_seen, so a price that returns to an earlier value is served as current and the current row is never pruned; the card computes its offers with its own DISTINCT ON over the product's variant ids (never through the view). The view and the card serve the latest row whatever its age or stock: freshness and stock filtering are S81-18's reader contract.
- **Merchants (R-S14-2, GC-3, GC-4, F1-3, F1-6):** `kb_merchants.registry jsonb` (ordered entries: an origin, an order, `source_router.Source` fields; `kb_service.build_merchant_row` validates origin, a unique int order, the present Source fields' types (a bool field refuses an int: F2-2) and domain == host (EXACT equality after lower-casing, ASCII only: no sibling-subdomain, apex or registrable-name shortcut, F2-3), and S81-15's seed calls it, never a copy; presence of every Source field is S81-15's seed contract, so an entry with no Source field passes this validator), every host, and the host of every URL column of every kb_* table, has a last label that carries a letter and is neither all digits nor 0x-hex (F1-6, F2-4: one regex, the leaf's `KB_HOST_TOP_LABEL_REGEX`, applied by the leaf and by the seven `*_top_label_letter` constraints on the SEVEN belted columns kb_merchants.host, kb_offers.canonical_url, kb_images.url, kb_images.provenance_host, kb_specs.source_url, kb_reviews.source_url and kb_gaps.source_hint, so 10.0.0.1, 127.0.0.0x1, 0x7f.0x0.0x0.0x1 and 1.2.3.0x4 are refused everywhere; a digit registrable label such as 4711.com or 163.com stays valid; `kb_merchants.search_template`, a display / ops URL template that is never decoded, carries NO belt (RB-6: a template column is not a URL column, so the 045 header's every-URL-column sentence stands)), `platform_confidence` text (mechanism | probe | url | operator), `status` (candidate | live | blocked), `deny`, `blocked_reason`; NO deny check, trigger or builder rule on kb_merchants: a denied host is STORED with deny = true and status blocked (the owner's blocked list; S81-15 never writes deny = false on such a row); the decode reads host, registry, status, deny, blocked_reason and partner only (country, currency_hint, platform, adapter, search_template are display / ops columns).
- **Deny rule (R-S14-6, R-S14-7):** ONE rule, `deny_hosts.kb_source_allowed`, applied by the builders and read at call time; the 045 CHECKs on the data tables' URL and host columns (and on `kb_gaps.source_hint`, a host or a canonical URL only, never free text) are BELTS derived from sorted(`deny_hosts.DENY_LABELS`) (basenotes, fimgs, fragrantica, parfumo) and a strict SUBSET of the rule (F1-5: a belt hit is always a whole-token hit of the rule, so a belt never refuses a row a builder accepted; the rule also refuses path and query tokens, hyphenated labels and top-level labels); `canonical_offer_url` keeps scheme + lower-case host + path, drops query and fragment, and refuses userinfo, a non-default port, an IP literal (dotted, or any all-digit or 0x-hex last label), a non-ASCII (IDN) host (punycode is accepted) and a deny-labelled host.
- **Privacy (R-S14-4, R-S14-15):** no users FK and no user / email / address / fingerprint column (the 043 fence sees no kb_* table; the column is `audience`, not `gender_marker`); `kb_feedback.device_key` is NULLABLE and pseudonymous (the S81-05 HMAC-SHA256 of the device header under `DEVICE_KEY_PEPPER`, not a user id and not the raw header), outside the 043 user-table fence by design; its deletion is S81-22's account-deletion hook (its compare_request_id filter is served by the index `idx_kb_feedback_request`, F2-6) plus the 365-day prune `kb_prune_feedback(365)` from S81-22's nightly job, and S81-12 discloses it (R-S12-18); a device-less vote is stored and never counts toward one vote per device; a pepper rotation or the S81-08 id change gives one device two keys. `kb_specs.lang` (en | ar, UNIQUE product_id, field, lang): the card serves cited specs only, in `p_lang` with an English fallback (R-S14-13).
- **Stated limits:** inert until the owner applies 045 / 047 AND a later flagged unit writes or reads; the GTIN check is format plus check digit only; one-letter or alias-only names mint no id (S81-16's curated aliases) and Emporio / Giorgio Armani share one brand axis; a generic but non-placeholder brand (Generic, No Brand, Brand Collection) still mints an id; identity edge outcomes (pinned or measured): "7.5ml sample" mints a DIFFERENT juice `...|sample-sauvage|EDP` sized 5, "100ml + 10ml travel spray" mints `...|plus-sauvage|EDP` sized 10, "Tester" gives `sauvage-tester`, "1.5ml vial" gives `sauvage-vial` at 5 ml, and a "Gift Set" collapses into the 100 ml bottle's SKU (a set price would be stored as a bottle offer); ids are fragrance-only; the card counts a rating-only source in `sources` but never in `rating` and keeps count-only rows in `review_count` without a rating; kb_offers grows until `kb_prune_offers` runs (S81-19 / D17 schedule it); ratings are stored on a 0..5 scale; PostgreSQL 15 or later; 047's rollback re-opens the anon write path on the L2 cache; the six APPLY files live in `.qa-s80/` of the unit worktree until the orchestrator copies them into the state folder at merge, and no committed test pins them; the leaf stays off `.github/black-clean-paths.txt` (CI's repo-wide black step is report-only, and a reformat would break the mutation table's edit texts; F1-12).
- **Owner steps (names only; a dated ledger line per step):** (1) run `APPLY_045_1_PRECHECK.sql` and send the grid; (2) `APPLY_045_2_ONE_PASTE.sql` (byte-identical to 045); (3) `APPLY_045_3_POSTCHECK.sql` (first apply only; part 1 runs the A/B/A offer check, the sale tie-break (S-b: a list price changed during an ongoing sale), the all-digit / 0x-hex host refusals on every host and URL column and the behaviour checks inside BEGIN ... ROLLBACK and leaves no row, part 2 is the privilege grid) and send the grid; 045 goes on before any kb_* writer flag flips (S81-15 / S81-16 / S81-17); (4) `APPLY_047_1_PRECHECK.sql` (the live pg_policies read), send it, and the orchestrator confirms the eight policy names; (5) `APPLY_047_2_ONE_PASTE.sql`; (6) `APPLY_047_3_POSTCHECK.sql` and send the grid. Measured by GREEN on a throwaway local PostgreSQL 18.1 cluster (2026-10-24, loopback only): 045 and 047 apply, re-apply, roll back and re-apply cleanly, every check file returns its expected grid, and the 045 POSTCHECK fails on a 045 whose view ranks captured_at first (the BL1 defect); re-measured by fix round 1 on the fix bytes (a fresh loopback 18.1 cluster, 2026-10-24): the same, plus the sale served over a list row of one instant (view, card, prune), the all-digit hosts refused, and the POSTCHECK failing on a 045 without the sale key or without the top-label constraint; re-measured by fix round 2 (a fresh loopback 18.1 cluster, 2026-10-24): the S-b case serves the sale in the view and the card and kb_prune_offers(1, 1) keeps it, every all-digit / 0x-hex host is refused on all seven host and URL columns (4711.com, 163.com accepted), and the POSTCHECK fails on a 045 with the fix-round-1 key order, the fix-round-1 merchant regex or without the kb_reviews belt; on 60,000 offers the card's offers read uses `idx_kb_offers_latest` (0.3 ms).

**SESSION 81 flags (S81-06 ANTHROPIC-ROUTER, 2026-10-24; default OFF, read PER CALL; new names, so unset in production by construction (Railway not read); PLAN_S81 M2 unit S81-06 (lane A); spec `.qa-s80/S81-06_UNIT_SPEC.md` of the unit worktree with its adversarial review (B1-B4, C1-C23), the rulings `specs/RULINGS_S81_06.md` (R-S06-1..R-S06-18), the RED gate `specs/GATE_S81_06.md` (GC-1..GC-11) and the fix-round rulings `specs/RULINGS_FIX1_S81_06.md` (F1-1..F1-20, 2026-10-24) in `docs/investigations/2026-10-22-session-81-state/`):**
- `ENABLE_ANTHROPIC_TEXT` / `ENABLE_ANTHROPIC_VISION` / `ENABLE_ANTHROPIC_VERDICT` / `ENABLE_ANTHROPIC_CRITIC` / `ENABLE_ANTHROPIC_MODERATION` + `ENABLE_SONNET_ESCALATION` + `ENABLE_OPENAI_PRE_V3` (S81-06, `app/services/llm_router.py`: `role_enabled` / `escalation_enabled` / `openai_pre_v3_enabled`, the house idiom) + knobs `ANTHROPIC_MAX_RETRIES` (2), `ANTHROPIC_CALL_DEADLINE_SECONDS` (25.0), `ANTHROPIC_CLASSIFY_LLM_TIMEOUT` (4.0), `ANTHROPIC_VISION_TIMEOUT_SECONDS` (20.0), `ANTHROPIC_TAIL_RESERVE_SECONDS` (6.5) (unset / blank / garbage / inf / nan / <= 0 -> the default), `ANTHROPIC_MODEL_TEXT` / `_VISION` / `_VERDICT` / `_CRITIC` / `_MODERATION` (default `claude-haiku-5-5`) and `ANTHROPIC_MODEL_ESCALATION` (default `claude-sonnet-5-5`) in `model_config`, and the credential NAME `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` (unset or blank = every anthropic role fails CLOSED, never a silent OpenAI fallback; in `tests/_env_safety.CREDENTIALS_UNSET` with `ANTHROPIC_AUTH_TOKEN` and `ANTHROPIC_BASE_URL`). **Effect ON:** each of the 16 chat dispatch sites (extraction_service x8, openai_service x5, image_service, url_extraction_service, verdict_critique_service) calls `_llm_router.route("<site>", _llm_breaker.guarded_llm_create, client, <the unchanged OpenAI kwargs>)`; with the site's role flag ON the request's `X-AI-Consent-Version` (S81-05 `consent_version()`) decides: >= 3 -> Claude through the ONE chokepoint `guarded_anthropic_create` (in order: the key check with no I/O, the S81-04 spend admit on lane `live` with the provider-aware bound incl. the output_config format bytes, its own `circuit:anthropic` breaker with every Redis call through `asyncio.to_thread` under 0.5 s (fail-open, one WARNING by type; a memo of `LLM_BREAKER_CACHE_TTL`), the call under `asyncio.wait_for(ANTHROPIC_CALL_DEADLINE_SECONDS)` on a memoised `AsyncAnthropic(api_key=<explicit>, max_retries=ANTHROPIC_MAX_RETRIES, base_url="https://api.anthropic.com")`, exceptions classified by CLASS and status (429 / 529 / >= 500 / connect / timeout transient -> a breaker failure; 400 / 401 / 403 / 404 / 409 / 413 / 422 / 402 terminal) and raised as `AnthropicDispatchError` (a constant message, `from None`; a client-construction failure of `_client_for` / the factory, e.g. an ImportError or a constructor error, is a terminal `AnthropicDispatchError(<type name>, None, False)` with a held half-open probe released and one WARNING by type, F1-7), the S81-03 llm-ledger record and `record_soon` BEFORE a `stop_reason refusal` raises `AnthropicRefusalError`) returning an OpenAI-shaped `ClaudeChatResult` so every reader works unchanged; consent absent / < 3 -> the exact OpenAI call while `ENABLE_OPENAI_PRE_V3` is ON, else `ConsentUpdateRequiredError`. Claude request shape: no temperature / top_p / store / response_format, `thinking` disabled (Sonnet escalation `between_tools`), `output_config` effort low, the per-site caps as constants in `SITES` (S81-07 re-measures); closed `output_config.format` schemas at image_tier3, vision_v1 (`{"products": [...]}` unwrapped to the array the v1 parser reads), vision_v2 (the B3 schema) and disambiguate (the `"true" / "false" / "unknown"` enum); the eleven prose-JSON sites (parse, specs, price, price_training, reviews, verdict, verdict_fallback, specs_targeted, specs_synthesized, url_extract, critique) take NO format, a JSON-only system line and a Claude-branch fence strip (GC-6 (b): their parsers read open dicts whose keys vary by category or prompt); image parts become image blocks (jpeg / png / gif / webp; with Pillow importable downscaled to 2,576 px and re-encoded JPEG q85, else refused above 5,000,000 bytes: production has no Pillow). The two text compare entries, `/url/compare` (both verbs, BEFORE the credit reserve) and the camera route (BEFORE `identify_products`, for ANY role it reaches) refuse a request an ON role would refuse (consent < 3 with PRE_V3 OFF, or anthropic with the key unset) with the existing coded 503 `LLM_UNAVAILABLE` (build 3 renders the engine-unavailable copy; refund as today, no history row); under ANY role flag ON a verdict-site `LLMUnavailableError` is a NON-DELIVERY (R-S06-2: `generate_comparison` re-raises, REST / stream / URL / camera map it to `LLM_UNAVAILABLE`), the four dispatch-level refusal log sites (parse, verdict, camera vision, url extract) log at WARNING by type, the moderation role is a Haiku classifier that fails CLOSED on EVERY failure class (a refusal, any dispatch failure incl. a client-construction error, malformed JSON and any other exception in the classifier step -> blocked, F1-7), ONE Sonnet escalation per compare on a refusal or a schema failure at a JSON site (lane `escalation`), the preflight (`ENABLE_LLM_PREFLIGHT_BREAKER`) is the AND of the breakers of every provider the ON roles decide (text, verdict; critic when `ENABLE_SELF_CRITIQUE` is ON; moderation when it routes to Claude), the scope (a fresh llm ledger + the escalation count) is bound in the parent task at both entries and the camera route (whose Claude records merge into `metadata.llm`), a Claude verdict skips the gpt-4o daily counter and the vision / disambiguate costs read the Claude list price, the comparison cache key gains `:anthropic:<model>` only while the verdict routes to Claude, `/health` gains `anthropic_breaker` {state, memo_age_seconds} only once a role flag was ON in the process, and Sentry carries the explicit `AnthropicIntegration(include_prompts=False)` (omitted with ONE WARNING when the SDK cannot import).
